Abstract (英文): Transparent-interface reconstruction must recover glass surfaces even when most observed color comes from the scene behind them. We present ClearForm, a shared-planar-Gaussian method for jointly reconstructing transparent interfaces and the appearance seen through them. We identify and analyze the Geometry-Appearance Compositing Dilemma (GACD): when the same transparency-dependent alpha controls both color and geometry, revealing the background can weaken a transparent surface's contribution to geometric reconstruction. We derive its effect on plane-based depth estimation, identify a mechanism that can weaken geometric learning, and quantify the loss of interface support through controlled comparisons. To address this conflict, we develop geometry-preserving dual-opacity surface reconstruction. Shared planar Gaussians retain interface contributions in geometry compositing while modeling transmission and sparse reflection in RGB, supported by transparency-aware geometric constraints. To manage the different primitive-visibility requirements of these compositors, we introduce Dual-Evidence Lifecycle (DEL), which retains Gaussians supported by multi-view geometry or RGB reconstruction evidence. On 3D-FRONT-T, ClearForm achieves 27.05 mm Chamfer distance (CD) and 0.928 F1, compared with 59.18 mm CD for our PGSR reproduction and the reported 34 mm CD and 0.836 F1 for GLINT. On TransLab, it achieves 1.65 mm CD, 0.963 F1, and 40.14 dB PSNR. In matched ablations, dual-opacity compositing reduces CD from 59.25 to 27.09 mm, while DEL improves PSNR by 2.06 dB. Our project page is available at https://hajeongx.github.io/clearform/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Modern dehazing networks are often computationally expensive and overfit to synthetic haze, limiting real-time deployment. This underscores an urgent demand for efficient, high-fidelity restoration methods that can operate on resource-constrained edge devices in practical, dynamic environments. We introduce PhysDNet, a lightweight multimodal architecture that fuses NIR imagery with sparse LiDAR depth and is trained with a physics-constrained dual-head objective. Differentiating through a fixed Koschmieder inversion induces density-adaptive gradient amplification, acting as an implicit resource allocation mechanism that concentrates learning on heavily degraded regions without increasing inference cost. PhysDNet delivers superior restoration with minimal capacity and enhanced training efficiency, facilitating real-time deployment on edge devices without increasing inference overhead. On the Seeing Through Fog benchmark, PhysDNet outperforms competitors by 1.67 dB at 5.2 times lower compute while recovering 54.9% of fog-induced detection loss. Ablations and cross-domain evaluations show that physics-guided training improves generalization, including under non-homogeneous haze that violates the Koschmieder assumption. PhysDNet further demonstrates compatibility with fixed-function NPUs, running at 29/45/99 FPS at <4 W across L/M/S variants. Code, deployment scripts and pre-trained checkpoints are available at https://github.com/Heatdh/PhysDnet.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Wildland urban interface (WUI) fires increasingly threaten communities and infrastructure, motivating the need for rapid visual assessment. Recent research has focused primarily on detecting fire and assessing post-event damage. However, structure-level fire threat identification during active WUI fire events remains underexplored. This study evaluates Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct for this active-fire decision using two separately scored tracks: 1,824 synthetic images from 491 designed WUI scenarios and 45 frames from a controlled-burn experiment conducted in collaboration with Columbus Fire & EMS Department. Under zero-shot prompting, the model returned valid target fields for 1,808 synthetic images and 39 controlled-burn frames. Accuracy among valid outputs was 94.0% for the synthetic track and 69.2% for the controlled-burn track, compared with a 16.0% random-selection baseline for the latter. On the synthetic track, the model generally identified the intended target across varied designed WUI scenarios when the relevant fire-spread cues were visually distinct. Controlled-burn performance was well above random selection, but errors showed difficulty interpreting spatial relations in complex scenes. The dual-track protocol provides scalable stress testing in designed scenarios and a controlled-burn reference for assessing model behavior under physical fire conditions. Future work can use the synthetic data for VLM post-training and then evaluate the adapted models on larger and more diverse real WUI fire datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Open-vocabulary 3D scene understanding aims to segment and retrieve arbitrary objects in 3D scenes using natural language queries. Most existing methods typically follow a splat-then-segment paradigm, where 3D Gaussian Splatting is first reconstructed and then augmented with labels or language features for subsequent segmentation. However, this pipeline often suffers from view inconsistency, ambiguous object boundaries, and weak object-level correspondence, especially in complex static and dynamic scenes. Building on our prior Segment-then-Splat framework, we present RefineSplat, a reconstruction-guided extension that retains its object-centric paradigm: it first tracks objects in 2D and then reconstructs each object with a dedicated set of 3D Gaussians, preserving explicit object-Gaussian correspondence for unified open-vocabulary segmentation and retrieval. To improve robustness, we further close the loop between 2D tracking and 3D reconstruction. Since off-the-shelf video trackers can produce unreliable masks that propagate errors into object reconstruction, we introduce a reconstruction-guided feedback mechanism that refines object tracking using rendered masks and multi-view consistency. Moreover, instead of assigning each object a single language embedding, we propose a viewpoint-aware embedding assignment strategy, where each object maintains multiple view-clustered embeddings to capture view-dependent semantic cues. We also introduce Blender-OVS, a synthetic benchmark of static and dynamic sequences with exact object meshes and point clouds, enabling direct evaluation of extracted geometry. Experiments on static and dynamic benchmarks show improved rendered-view segmentation and lower object-level Chamfer distances, with competitive scene reconstruction quality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): LLM agents have made impressive progress in coding and mathematical reasoning, motivating growing interest in learning from long-horizon interaction. Memory supports this process, but plausible lessons can be redundant or reinforce inappropriate actions. Can a memory's source decision provide a practical test for whether to retain it? We localize consequential trajectory steps, extract candidate memories, and replay each source decision with and without its candidate under the same context. This reveals two properties: most candidates barely shift the probed distribution, and both successful and failed source actions can be reinforced or suppressed. These observations motivate Anchor, which tests shift magnitude and source-role alignment before retention. Admitted memories guide later trajectories, enabling experience-driven improvement under a frozen policy. On held-out ALFWorld and WebShop tasks, Anchor improves success rate and mean score by up to 19.9% and 17.2%, respectively, relative to the strongest baseline for each backbone. With Qwen3.5-27B, it retains 41% fewer ALFWorld cards than unfiltered retention and outperforms all four WebShop cost-comparison baselines while using fewer tokens.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World models enable planning through internal simulation, while natural language provides intuitive task specification. Yet, language-conditioned world modeling remains fragmented across domains with disparate action spaces, datasets, and evaluation protocols. We introduce LCWM, a shared task that maps an initial agent-view observation and a language instruction to an action sequence that fulfills the instruction. Its accompanying benchmark, LCWM-Bench, integrates 15 datasets across navigation, robotics, 3D gaming, and driving under a shared action interface and evaluation protocol, pairing 142,987 episodes with 428,961 instructions in three styles. We further propose Tiresias, an agent that plans via selective imagination: a language-conditioned diffusion policy generates candidate trajectories, a trajectory ranker shortlists them, and a video-pretrained world model imagines their visual consequences. A visual ranker evaluates these rollouts, complementing trajectory scores to select the final plan. Tiresias achieves competitive planning and imagination performance on LCWM-Bench. Ablations demonstrate the complementary benefits of trajectory ranking and visual verification, while experiments with alternative proposal methods show that the trained rankers remain effective beyond our diffusion policy. Together, LCWM-Bench and Tiresias provide a unified evaluation setting and an effective starting point for studying language-guided planning through world models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing methods mainly rely on convolutional aggregation or attention-based spatial-spectral interaction. However, conventional feature-domain attention does not explicitly characterize variation patterns along spatial-token and learned latent-channel coordinates, while spatially shared local operators lack the flexibility to adapt neighborhood aggregation to heterogeneous structures. To address these limitations, we propose a Frequency-Structure Complementary Modeling Network (FSCM-Net), which couples frequency-aware dependency learning with structure-adaptive local refinement. Specifically, a Spatial-Spectral Frequency Calibration Attention (SSFCA) module constructs spatial-token affinities from Fourier-interacted query-key representations and builds latent-channel affinities after input-conditioned frequency filtering along learned channel coordinates. The latent-channel response is then calibrated by a learned shared gain and combined with the spatial-token response. Complementarily, a Structure-Coherent Dynamic Feature Fusion (SC-DFF) module combines regularized structure-tensor cues from MSI features with context-modulated HSI detail representations to generate channel- and position-dependent aggregation kernels. This design makes local HSI refinement responsive to smooth regions, edges, and textures while maintaining spectral fidelity. The two modules are progressively integrated within a multiscale encoder-decoder to coordinate representation-level interaction and structure-adaptive reconstruction. Experiments on two simulated benchmarks and one real remote-sensing dataset demonstrate favorable performance against representative fusion methods in spatial detail reconstruction and spectral preservation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unified whole-body pose estimators jointly predict body, face, and hand landmarks, but often degrade on small, blurred, or occluded hands. Attaching a standalone hand specialist introduces extra detection overhead, coordinate alignment, and wrist-stitching artifacts. We introduce S2HAND, a hand-specialized Sapiens2-5B framework that preserves the native 308-keypoint output interface. Specifically, S2HAND-MT adapts a lightweight 0.87M-parameter pose branch to route 40 finger heatmaps while strictly preserving the shared Transformer, auxiliary task heads, and the other 268 body, foot, and face heatmaps. S2HAND-Pose further fine-tunes three Fisher-selected Transformer blocks (267.13M parameters), trading exact preservation for stronger hand recovery: it lifts macro AUC from 57.83% to 65.73% across HInt TEST, COCO WholeBody VAL, and FreiHAND EVAL, and surpasses HInt-adapted Hand5/HRNet and stitched Hand5 systems on all five Native Pipeline benchmarks. On COCO, S2HAND-Pose improves hand AP by 12.89 points and body-plus-foot AP by 0.19 points. Across a 512-image multi-task audit, it maintains 99.60% foreground segmentation agreement with the public checkpoint, with quantified normal and pointmap drift. Its 2D coordinates also lower 3D mesh error in frozen Pose2Mesh transfer, while our optional TailGuard re-cropping module cuts S2HAND-MT's severe failure rate on NewDays from 13.45% to 10.43%. S2HAND thus resolves the whole-body hand bottleneck with either exact non-hand preservation (MT) or empirically audited trade-offs (Pose).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mental simulation, the ability to reason by constructing and updating internal visual states, is central to flexible human cognition, but remains elusive in current multimodal large language models. Latent multimodal reasoning offers a promising route toward this capability by interleaving text with continuous visual latent blocks, allowing models to maintain internal visual workspaces without decoding them into pixels. In this paper, we ask whether these latent visual states actually matter. Through a direct perturbation analysis across representative latent-reasoning MLLMs, we find that they often do not: dropping, replacing, or shuffling generated visual latents has only minor effects on the model's own final-answer probability, while equivalent perturbations to the linguistic reasoning trace are substantially more damaging. We call this failure mode Mental Simulation Collapse (MSC): the model appears to perform interleaved visual reasoning, but routes most answer-relevant computation through text. We further diagnose that MSC follows naturally from existing training objectives: visual alignment can make generated latents resemble helper-image embeddings, but it does not require the decoder to rely on them; subsequent text-only supervision further leaves the semantic role of the latent pathway unidentifiable. To address this, we introduce AnchorSim (Anchored Mental Simulation), a contrastive distillation framework that trains models to make their generated visual latents consequential. Across multiple visual reasoning benchmarks, AnchorSim improves performance, while diagnostic re-evaluation shows that visual perturbations now meaningfully affect predictions. Our results expose a fundamental failure mode of latent multimodal reasoning and demonstrate that mental simulation must be anchored to model behavior in order to matter.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual data are often expressed through multiple complementary modalities (e.g., images and segmentation masks), each capturing different aspects of the same underlying structure. Most existing multimodal methods adopt a top-down paradigm, learning a shared latent variable through joint training from scratch, which limits scalability. We propose Split and Bridge (SnB), a bottom-up framework for multimodal generation that couples pretrained unimodal diffusion models at sampling time. Rather than learning a unified latent space, SnB generates coherent multimodal samples by following a shared diffusion trajectory up to a split point and then branching into modality-specific trajectories guided by a bridge module. This design enables plug-and-play reuse of powerful pretrained models without joint training, making it scalable and easily adaptable. Across standard benchmarks (PolyMNIST, CelebAMask-HQ) and more complex settings (PIE-Bench++, ROCO), SnB achieves a strong balance between sample quality and coherence. We show that SnB naturally extends to downstream zero-shot tasks such as interleaved co-generation, image editing, and style coupling/decoupling. Our results demonstrate that bottom-up generation provides a practical framework that complements existing top-down multimodal approaches. Code is available at https://github.com/AhmadArrabi/SnB

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but attention over long spatiotemporal sequences makes inference computationally expensive. Sparse attention reduces this cost by computing only a subset of query-key interactions exactly, and recent training-free methods further approximate the remaining interactions. These methods, however, degrade quickly at high sparsity, which limits the achievable speedup. We present ESTRA (Extreme Sparsity Through Refined Attention), a training-free acceleration method that preserves generation quality even at extreme sparsity. ESTRA concentrates exact attention computation on a small set of important key-value tokens and approximates the remaining contributions with cluster representatives. To identify these tokens efficiently, it groups similar queries and uses their representatives to score individual keys. A variance-based correction further refines the attention weights of the cluster representatives, improving the accuracy of the approximation. Experiments on Wan2.1-14B and HunyuanVideo-13B show that ESTRA establishes a new quality-efficiency frontier for training-free sparse attention, achieving $3.26\times$ and $4.71\times$ end-to-end speedups at 5% attention density while surpassing Sol-Attn and SVG-EAR in fidelity to dense generation and on most VBench dimensions. Even at 1% density, ESTRA remains more faithful to dense generation than Sol-Attn at 12.5%, while reaching up to $5.39\times$ speedup.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Microsoft Word is a central environment for office automation, and the faithful execution of its editing operations is an important capability for document agents. Unlike plain-text editing, editing in Word modifies a structured object model, where the requested effect is realized in object properties. Existing benchmarks, however, evaluate office workflows or desktop automation, treating word-processor documents as one task domain among many and verifying outcomes with keyword matching, per-task property checks, or screenshot comparison. We introduce WordEval, an operation-fidelity benchmark that instead centers evaluation on Word editing operations, testing whether each requested edit is realized as intended changes to Word objects and properties. It contains 642 cases across 15 Word feature areas, ranging from text and lists to charts, shape geometry, and SmartArt. 546 atomic cases cover 231 commands, 96 compositional cases combine multiple edits in a single document, and a Word-native oracle checks task-specific object properties and preservation conditions. Cases are constructed from editing-unit specifications, yielding controlled input documents, reference documents, and aligned oracle checks. Each compositional case is linked to corresponding atomic cases, supporting fine-grained diagnosis of editing failures. We evaluate eight agent systems and find that many struggle on the Atomic track, with performance varying across feature areas. Moreover, every system scores lower on Compositional than on Atomic, with gaps that vary substantially by system. Exact atomic-intent links and recorded failure diagnoses provide complementary views of this variation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The task of visual reasoning relative pose identification (VRRPI) has exposed critical weaknesses in the multi-view 3D spatial reasoning capabilities of modern vision-language models (VLMs). We present a systematic analysis of VLMs' behavior in VRRPI, uncovering stable output biases where VLMs disproportionately predict specific motion patterns regardless of visual evidence. To mitigate these inherent biases, we propose self knowledge re-expression with iterative debiasing (SKR-ID), an annotation-free pipeline that adapts VLMs for VRRPI using only randomly extracted unannotated image pairs. SKR-ID transitions VLMs' output mechanism from generic next-token prediction to task-optimized classification heads. The pipeline proceeds in two stages: (1) a cold-start initialization using debiasing object-centric prompts to anchor motion inference in 2D visual evidence; and (2) a recursive logit-ranking mechanism that enforces statistical parity by using the median logit value as a dynamic threshold. Experimental evaluation across frontier VLMs on standard benchmarks demonstrates that SKR-ID significantly mitigates systematic biases. Our method improves open-source VLMs' Macro-F1 scores from 35.0-45.4% to 53.7-58.0%, without using any annotated data. These results show that SKR-ID can effectively improve 3D reasoning performance from a severely collapsed baseline. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/astudentuser/Codes_of_SKR-ID.

Abstract (英文): Background: Well-annotated, diverse medical imaging datasets remain a major constraint for vision-based medical AI. We evaluated Nano Banana, a general-purpose image generation and editing model, as a source of synthetic medical images for model training, subgroup augmentation, and model auditing. Methods: In this retrospective study across four imaging modalities, chest radiography, fundus photography, and CT models were trained only on synthetic images and externally validated on real-world data against real-data-trained models. Carotid ultrasound models were trained on a private Human Phenotype Project cohort (aged 40-75 years) with and without synthetic augmentation. Clinicians rated image plausibility. Evaluation used AUROC, AUPR, calibration, and decision curve analysis. Findings: Synthetic-only models achieved AUROCs of 0.877 (95% CI 0.843-0.910) for pneumonia (n=14863), 0.963 (0.948-0.975) for retinal detachment (n=1149), and 0.711 (0.600-0.823) for renal calculus (n=4122), comparable to real-data-trained models, but their calibration and net benefit were not consistently more favorable. With mixed synthetic-real training, a carotid ultrasound model produced an ordinal vascular age index that stratified 633 external subjects into subgroups differing in BMI and glucose (both p<0.01), linking vascular aging to cardiometabolic health. To stress-test shortcut learning, editing a moderate tessellated pattern into fundus photographs reduced the true-class probability for central serous chorioretinopathy from 0.996 to 0.822. Clinician-guided editing raised low-quality CT plausibility from 3.10/5 to 4.30/5. Interpretation: Synthetic data can complement real data in model training, subgroup representation, and model testing via controlled editing, but its clinical value should be assessed with calibration, decision curve analysis, and clinician review, not discrimination alone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image super-resolution in the dark is fundamentally challenged by extreme spatial heterogeneity: severely underexposed and noisy regions demand aggressive generative enhancement to reconstruct missing details, while relatively well-exposed areas require conservative updates to prevent hallucinated textures. Standard diffusion and flow-matching models, however, rely on globally uniform integration trajectories, making them sub-optimal for such spatially varying degradations. In this paper, we propose DeLang-SR (Degradation-Language steered Super-Resolution), a one-step flow-matching framework that explicitly decouples the restoration direction and magnitude. Rather than treating low-light corruption as arbitrary latent features, DeLang-SR translates measurable physical statistics into structured degradation language, harnessing the semantic prior of pretrained text-to-image models. The degradation-language intent, complemented by image-specific visual tokens, steers the velocity field (direction) toward restoration-relevant manifolds. Simultaneously, a continuous spatial update-strength field acts as a locally varying Euler step-size field (magnitude), applying stronger integration steps in heavily degraded shadows while enforcing conservative updates in reliable regions. Experiments on paired and real dark images, together with ablations and diagnostic analyses, show that DeLang-SR provides an effective and interpretable one-step route to super-resolution in the dark. Code and pretrained models are available at https://github.com/moriyaya/DeLang-SR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-channel images encode heterogeneous channel semantics aligned over a shared spatial structure, which requires models to jointly capture globally shared structure and channel-specific variations. Recent multi-channel Vision Transformers attempt to address this challenge by augmenting the [CLS] token with memory tokens to organize multi-channel representations under varying channel compositions. However, we find that these context tokens collapse onto a few dominant channels under high inter-channel redundancy, producing redundant representations and a biased global summary that fails to reliably support composition-aware channel interpretation. We propose MuCa-ViT (Multi-Channel Composition-Aware Vision Transformer), a unified framework that addresses these limitations through two coupled mechanisms. Factorial Token Learning (FTL) enforces token-level decorrelation among context tokens, encouraging the [CLS] token to capture globally shared semantics while memory tokens preserve complementary channel-specific cues. Channel Composition-Aware Modulation (CAM) uses the FTL-refined [CLS] as a semantic anchor to generate sample-wise modulation signals, which enable composition-aware interpretation of channels within a unified backbone rather than relying on static channel embeddings. Experiments on JUMP-CP, CHAMMI, and So2Sat show that MuCa-ViT outperforms prior multi-channel ViTs across all three benchmarks, and the magnitude of improvement aligns with the inter-channel redundancy of each dataset ($ρ\in [0.19, 0.77]$), validating the diagnostic analysis that motivates our design. Our code is available at https://github.com/kimdanni/MuCa-ViT .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Predicting where multiple agents will move next is fundamental to autonomous driving, robotics, and any system that must share space with people safely. Modern stochastic predictors read off inter-agent interactions once from the observed past and keep that view fixed throughout the generative rollout, so the reciprocal geometric constraints that emerge as the joint future sharpens are not re-examined. We address this gap with SRA (Spatial Reasoning Adapter), a modular graph denoiser that is integrated into existing diffusion and flow-matching predictors without redesigning their generative cores. At every reverse step, SRA reads the host's current future estimate, builds a sparse Euclidean interaction graph on the predicted trajectories, and returns a residual correction to the host's decoding path. Our adapter consists of three core components: a sparse neighborhood retrieved with a learned query-key score and a geometric bias, a future-conditioned relational feature recomputed on the evolving prediction, and uncertainty-weighted message passing where confident agents act as anchors for uncertain ones. We instantiate SRA on three structurally different hosts (LED, MID, and MoFlow) across four multi-agent benchmarks: SDD, NBA, Soccer, and Football. SRA improves ADE for every host on every dataset, with average gains of 5.8% in ADE and 7.0% in FDE. A cumulative ablation shows that the three components act as one mechanism: only together do they improve every host on every sports dataset, indicating that spatial reasoning over the model's own evolving future, weighted by how far that future can be trusted, is broadly useful across denoising architectures. Code is available at https://github.com/Jaewoo97/SRA.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present a hypernetwork that generates the parameters of an implicit neural representation (INR) for a given signal in a single forward pass, without test-time optimization. Existing hypernetworks for this problem either ignore the chain-rule structure of the test-time optimization that produces an INR or, when they preserve it, instantiate the structure with MLPs whose parameter count grows quartically in target-network width. Our central contribution is to show that this parameter-count cost is structural to the MLP instantiation, not to the optimization-oriented principle of preserving chain-rule structure, and to resolve it by replacing the MLP body with attention. The resulting hypernetwork combines three design choices: an output-neuron-centric tokenization that aligns the token axis with each layer's output-neuron axis; an information-flow consistency rule that fixes the role of every cross-attention module from the representation its output should occupy; and a token-space iterative rollout that realizes the five quantities one optimization step on the target INR computes as five cross-attention modules with parameter count independent of target-network width. Across five 2D and 3D INR generation benchmarks, our method outperforms the state-of-the-art single-pass and optimization-oriented baselines; scales to target-network widths at which the MLP-based optimization-oriented paradigm exhausts GPU memory; and produces the highest result on NeRF generation without ground-truth INR weights supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Stereo matchers estimate depth by comparing the left and right images from a stereo camera pair, but they assume that the two cameras are accurately calibrated and rectified. In real deployments, small physical shifts after calibration, caused by camera motion, mechanical drift, vibration, or installation changes, can break this assumption and degrade depth estimation. Online stereo rectification is therefore needed to correct the image pair during operation and recover a stereo geometry where standard stereo matching can work reliably. Recent online rectification methods mainly optimize row alignment, making the left and right views of the same scene point fall on the same image row. We show that this target, while necessary, is insufficient for deployable stereo depth estimation. A rectifier may make a small set of matched points look well row-aligned, but still distort the full images in ways that hurt stereo matching: useful image regions may be cut off, large blank areas may appear, objects may be stretched or squeezed, or image scale may change unevenly across the view. We propose VirtualRect, an online stereo rectification method designed for reliable real-world deployment. Rather than treating rectification as simply making the left and right image rows line up, this method estimates a rectified virtual stereo camera and combines image-wide supervision of image warp behavior. This makes the rectified images and the camera parameters come from the same geometry, so the images used for matching and the model used for depth computation remain consistent. Experiments on multiple datasets show that, compared with the prior state-of-the-art online rectifier, VirtualRect reduces vertical flow error by 17% on Carla-Flowguided and 7% on Semi-Truck Highway, and lowers depth MAE by 30-41% on Carla-Flowguided across three downstream stereo matchers.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Latent-action world models learn actions from video without labels, and recent methods aim to share these actions across bodies. Transfer between 2D characters with different body structures remains underexplored. In this paper, we study whether an action learned from one character can make a character with a different body perform the same action. A cat, a blob and a human can all jump, but each moves its body in its own way. The usual transfer gain mixes body motion with screen movement and can assign a perfect score to a model that ignores its action. To address this, we introduce a transfer score that compares the correct action with wrong ones from the same source. We test five training objectives on a new synthetic benchmark of 2D cartoon characters with six body plans, the standard Sprites benchmark, and 431 selected animal and human animations from DeformingThings4D. With a fixed camera, a baseline that only copies the source's screen movement scores 0.71, as high as most learned models. In contrast, with a camera that follows the body, the best model reaches only 0.46 for an unseen animal family and 0.20 for humans. With this camera, a linear probe identifies the action class of unseen animal families with 66% to 86% balanced accuracy (chance 17%). The learned actions therefore retain information about which action is performed, but the correct action closes only part of the prediction error gap for a new character.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) routinely disclose soft-biometric attributes such as age, gender, and race in their text outputs, exposing demographic information from uploaded images to any downstream API user. Existing privacy defenses do not fit this setting: face de-identification leaves distributed soft-biometric cues intact, classifier-targeted adversarial perturbations do not transfer to open-ended generation, and stronger VLM-specific methods require retraining or runtime activation access unavailable to downstream APIs. We propose PrivMark, a framework that suppresses biometric leakage at the image level. By analyzing VLM activations, we identify two weakly aligned steering vector directions in hidden-state space, each tied to a distinct leakage behavior --- one corresponding to incidental attribute disclosure under open-ended descriptions, the other to compliance with direct biometric probes instead of refusal --- and encode both directions into a single bounded pixel perturbation, so the protected image alone enforces privacy with no invasive access to model internals at deployment. To support evaluation, we assemble BioleakBench, $1{,}600$ VQA pairs covering biometric privacy attributes under both free-form description and direct-probe protocols. Across three white-box source models, PrivMark achieves up to $85.9\%$ biometric suppression while retaining $75\%$ of clean OK-VQA utility, up to $1.4\times$ the privacy-utility tradeoff of the strongest pixel-only baseline. The same one-shot perturbations transfer consistently to seven unseen VLMs including closed-source APIs.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cinematic composition questions depend on in-frame geometry that vision-language models (VLMs) use inconsistently. External perception tools can expose this geometry, but naive prompt injection can overwrite useful visual predictions. On ShotBench composition, pre-question geometry reduces Qwen2.5-VL-7B from 49.5% to 46.3%; corrupted measurements lower it to 33.6%. CompositionEvidence derives nine reproducible features from frozen detection, depth, and line experts and supplies their shared 24-dimensional representation through two interfaces. A training-free channel appends gated neutral evidence after the question, reaching 56.2% (+6.7, exact McNemar p=0.0018) and 48.4% under corrupted evidence. A minimal-training channel maps the same representation to four soft tokens with a 7.37M-parameter projector while leaving the VLM frozen; it reaches 61.0% (+11.5). Across nine VLMs, including five commercial models queried through official provider APIs, zero-shot accuracy ranges from 43.8% to 63.0%, and the text channel changes it by +6.7 to -6.9 points; the highest baseline is among the regressions. The effect of explicit geometry depends on measurement reliability and on how the receiving model integrates it.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent large human reconstruction models create remarkable animatable 3D avatars from a single image, but their appearance remains entangled with the illumination and cannot be relighted. In contrast, existing 3D relightable-avatar reconstruction methods typically require person-specific optimization. We present ZARA, a zero-shot feed-forward framework that reconstructs an animatable 3D Gaussian avatar together with its intrinsic attributes from a single image. ZARA couples a pretrained large-scale human reconstruction model with a Material Transformer to inherit geometry priors while learning material-aware Gaussian representations. To preserve localized details, we introduce Gaussian-aligned material feature fusion strategy, which injects multi-scale material features onto individual Gaussians and combines them with geometry tokens. As most human-avatar datasets lack ground-truth material maps, we use pseudo-material labels as conditioning inputs and supervision targets during training. We further introduce MatFace regularization to preserve facial structure and color in albedo prediction under unreliable facial pseudo-material supervision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ZARA reconstructs animatable and relightable humans within tens of seconds without person-specific optimization or post-processing, outperforming existing baselines in both relighting quality and materials generalization ability. As the ability of recovering the physically-based-rendering attributes of the human, ZARA also enables direct material editing of the avatar's appearance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual Attention enhances convolutional neural networks (CNNs) by modulating intermediate features, yet their effects on downstream decisions remain less understood. We present a decision-space framework that models multiplicative attention as an intermediate feature perturbation and derives a first-order prediction of its effect on the target-versus-competitor classification margin. We further introduce the Decision Alignment Score (DAS) to quantify the directional alignment between the perturbation and the local margin gradient. We evaluate the framework on CIFAR-100 using SimAM and CBAM across five popular CNN backbones, ResNet-18, ResNet-20, ResNet-50, ResNet-56, and MobileNetV2. The analysis examines prediction agreement, DAS margin correlations, and downstream activation-state changes. Across most final-attention configurations, predicted and actual margin changes show good agreement, while DAS exhibits positive correlations with actual margin changes. Single-attention experiments further show that prediction agreement and DAS margin correlations depend on attention placement, with stronger associations at later stages.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) can recognize events in images and parse sentences, but it remains unclear when they bind visual Agent-Patient structure to linguistic semantic roles across languages whose role cues depend on morphology, word order or construction. We introduce ROLECUE, a controlled diagnostic that pairs 192 validated two-person action images with German, Turkish, Japanese, English, Korean and Bangla sentences in canonical and patient-first orders. The evaluation separates image-only role recognition, text-only role parsing, direct image-sentence matching, response-compliance behavior and an external modular composition of the model's own component predictions. Across eight recent open-weight VLMs, ROLECUE reveals a structured spectrum: some models fail visual or linguistic component tests, some largely solve the diagnostic, some collapse to constant semantic assent and several show component-joint gaps in which reliable image-only and text-only role predictions do not survive direct joint inference. The largest non-degenerate gaps reach 44.9 percentage points, with final bootstrap intervals confirming that the effect is not a point-estimate artifact. These results reveal multilingual component-joint performance gaps and acceptance biases hidden by aggregate VLM benchmarks and show why component diagnostics are necessary before interpreting joint multimodal errors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models can generate motion graphics from static images and natural language requests, but it remains difficult to evaluate how well models can follow requests that combine spatial, appearance, and temporal requirements. We introduce MotionGraphicsBench, an expert-curated benchmark of 100 instances for animating an input static SVG with a motion edit prompt. By supplying all required visual elements, our editing task reflects a designer-like workflow and isolates motion generation from graphic generation. Derived from real-world Lottie motion designs, our benchmark contains diverse combinations of spatio-temporal animation behaviors. Each benchmark instance pairs its model input with a deterministic, human-readable verification program that accepts different valid implementations and reports which prompt requirements pass or fail, rather than comparing against a reference animation or using a VLM judge. We construct these programs with a multi-agent pipeline using human-labeled animations and synthesized test cases. On a balanced test set of 180 animations, our programs achieve 93.3% raw agreement with human judgments, compared with 83.3% for a GPT-6 Astra VLM baseline, and outperform the baseline in both approval and rejection recall. Across 47 configurations from 32 LLMs and 12 providers, pass@1 ranges from 6.0% to 67.7%. Across 15 models evaluated at their highest available reasoning effort with 32 attempts per instance, additional attempts improve every model, but pass@32 still ranges from 38.0% to 95.0%. Our analysis of verification programs reveals that generated animations often fail to match reference poses, follow requested motion trajectories, or coordinate related events. We release our benchmark at https://huggingface.co/datasets/figma-ai/motion-graphics-bench.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative AI can expand cultural collections into prompt-responsive visual media, but historical artworks rarely provide the paired descriptions needed to adapt modern image generators. We present The RenAIssance, a scalable pipeline that turns a collection of historical European artworks into a reusable text-to-image media resource. The pipeline generates scene-level captions with BLIP and uses the resulting approximately 15,000 image--text pairs from DEArt to train a compact LoRA adapter for Stable Diffusion v1.5. The adapter shifts generated images toward the historical-art distribution while retaining the base model's ability to render novel concepts. On historical and contemporary prompts of different lengths, it improves FID to the DEArt reference distribution in every setting with only a modest decrease in CLIP text--image similarity. Qualitative results show that the same adapter can render both period-appropriate subjects and contemporary concepts in a coherent historical visual language. By decoupling a reusable style representation from the base generator, the approach offers a practical building block for controllable historical-art media creation, interactive authoring, and rich-media experiences.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long video understanding requires models to identify and reason over sparse, temporally distributed evidence within limited frame budgets. Existing keyframe selection methods typically retrieve query-relevant frames from a densely sampled candidate set before inference. However, global image-text matching incurs unnecessary computation when a small set of uniformly sampled frames already provides sufficient evidence. Query relevance alone may also overlook evidence that distinguishes competing answers, while one-shot selection offers no opportunity to correct earlier selection errors. We propose HypoKFS, a training-free, plug-and-play framework that couples keyframe selection with iterative inference by multimodal large language models (MLLMs). HypoKFS first reasons over sparsely sampled frames to construct an initial hypothesis space. The resulting answer distribution determines whether additional frame selection is needed. When needed, the retained hypotheses guide the selection of discriminative frames, followed by renewed inference and hypothesis refinement. This iterative process directs additional visual computation toward unresolved hypotheses while avoiding unnecessary global frame matching. Experiments on Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple MLLM backbones. HypoKFS improves accuracy by up to 6.7 percentage points and achieves 72.9% accuracy on Video-MME at only 1.3% of the reference frame-hypothesis matching cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Endoscopic diagnosis unfolds over native video, where a single positive finding during traversal of the gastrointestinal tract sets off a multi-minute chain of reasoning over location, morphology, severity and intervention. Existing endoscopy multimodal benchmarks test only static images or single-disease videos, which miss the temporal and clinical reasoning that real-world reporting demands. We introduce EndoSCOP-V, a video-grounded multi-turn evaluation framework for structured endoscopy reporting and clinical reasoning. It contains 1,307 real-world endoscopy and colonoscopy videos (63.2 h; 97% 1080p) averaging about 174 s. Its 20,274 question-answer turns are instantiated deterministically from clinician-filled structured endoscopy-reporting EHRs, so no per-case annotation is synthetic or LLM-rephrased. They come from 1,033 clinician-reviewed templates, about 18 per disease, that span a clinically aligned 9-task x 30-sub-task reporting taxonomy over 54 World Endoscopy Organization (WEO) MST 3.0 diseases. 22% of procedures carry co-existing pathologies, up to six per case. A Markovian context-pruned oracle-forcing protocol evaluates the dialogue turn by turn, injecting the gold answer before each subsequent turn so that per-turn reasoning is isolated from cascading dialogue errors. A panel of 15 board-certified endoscopists sets a 78% strict-accuracy baseline on 1,500 questions. Across 14 open-weight LMMs, the best 27B-class model reaches 59.8% strict accuracy, falling to 53.4% once format-trivial disease-rejection items are excluded. On a between-subjects spot-check, the best of 3 frontier proprietary models trails the clinician panel by 23 pp on strict NDR accuracy. Sub-task analysis reveals consistent gaps in quantitative measurement, multi-variable grading and intervention reasoning on long-horizon video. Data: Hugging Face (EndoSCOP-V). Code: GitHub (abhi25t/EndoSCOP-V).

</details>

Abstract (英文): We introduce Latent-WAM++, a representation-centered World Action Model (WAM) for end-to-end autonomous driving. Existing WAMs are primarily trained to predict future states or final actions, leaving the intermediate world and action representations that connect prediction to planning only indirectly constrained. Latent-WAM++ matches the supervision of each representation to its role in planning. World Representation Construction combines scene-ego future prediction, SIGReg-based distribution stabilization, and frozen geometric priors to learn a temporally predictive, non-degenerate, and geometry-aware world representation without an EMA target encoder. Trajectory MeanFlow Planner directly predicts multimodal trajectory proposals while being optimized with an Improved MeanFlow velocity-space objective, enabling one-step sampling. Visual-Action Representation Alignment further aligns the resulting pre-output action features with a stop-gradient target derived from current visual semantics. Latent-WAM++ achieves state-of-the-art performance on NAVSIM v2 and under zero-shot evaluation on HUGSIM, reaching 90.1 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2 and 47.0 average RC and 30.1 average HD-Score on HUGSIM. Training the planner end to end costs 190 GPU-hours for Latent-WAM++ versus 1,600 for Latent-WAM, a reduction of 88.1%.

Abstract (英文): Full projector compensation adjusts a projector's input image to compensate for both geometric and photometric distortions introduced by the projection surface. Existing methods generally treat the viewing condition as a static camera viewpoint. When applied to a moving viewer, existing methods face two key challenges: (1) view-dependent methods require model retraining or recalibration to compensate for novel viewpoints; and (2) NeRF/GS-based view-agnostic methods use structure from motion (SfM) to calibrate the projector-camera systems (ProCams) with an unknown global scale, while view-aware tracking requires calibrating a camera oriented opposite to the projector, which introduces an additional calibration challenge. To address these challenges, we introduce VAComp, the first view-aware framework for full projector compensation for a moving viewer in a naked-eye setting. Using a projector, VAComp enables the viewer to perceive a virtual object that visually replaces the real object or to observe view-consistent modifications of the real object's appearance and material properties. To resolve the SfM scale ambiguity, we use metric calibration as a reference to recover the global scale; to calibrate the oppositely facing camera, we use two checkerboards, one facing the RGB camera and the other facing the depth camera. To balance compensation quality and runtime efficiency, we develop two non-iterative hybrid methods, VAComp-SD and VAComp-SI. Both combine 2D Gaussian-based view-conditioned geometric correction with learned photometric compensation, predicting each compensation image through a single forward pass without per-frame iterative optimization. Real-world experiments show that both methods achieve competitive compensation quality, with VAComp-SD enabling real-time updates and VAComp-SI operating at interactive rates. Project page: https://realkaizhou.github.io/VAComp/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual citations show where an answer is supported, but not how the cited content contributes to it. In scientific document question answering, a model may read a value directly, compress several observations, or combine evidence to infer a conclusion. We study typed visual provenance, where a system generates an answer together with source regions and explicit support relations. We introduce ReViE, a dataset of 3,941 questions over complete scientific documents, with page, section, and region annotations labeled as Quotation, Compression, or Inference. We further propose VisProve, which augments joint answer and provenance generation with differentiable Spatial Evidence Reading and Evidence-Answer Relation Feedback. VisProve conditions next-token prediction on localized visual evidence and its estimated support relation through a gated norm-bounded residual. On the document-disjoint ReViE test set, VisProve improves Typed-F1 from 3.52 to 5.48 and Section-F1 from 45.99 to 53.24 over joint supervised fine-tuning with the same backbone, while Page-F1 changes only marginally. These results suggest that coupling visual grounding with support relation modeling mainly improves fine-grained provenance, while the low absolute Typed-F1 reveals a substantial gap between answer generation and precise visual evidence attribution.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AI agents can generate as-built Building Information Models (BIMs) from reality-capture data, but the evidence required for accurate reconstruction remains unclear. This paper presents Agent2BIM, an agent-based Scan-to-BIM framework, and evaluates seven capture configurations in a research laboratory and a mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) scene. Inputs include 360° panoramas, smartphone video, smartphone LiDAR, terrestrial laser scanning (TLS), TLS with posed panoramas, and imagery supplemented by prior IFC models. Outputs are compared against TLS-verified reference models for component detection, geometric accuracy, and MEP connectivity. In the laboratory, component recall ranged from 84% to 100%, but only 9% of components reconstructed from panoramas were within 20 cm of their reference positions, compared with 86% using smartphone LiDAR. In the MEP scene, scale-free inputs recovered 11 - 43% of components, versus 75 - 93% for metric inputs. Prior IFC models improved MEP reconstruction but could propagate inaccuracies. The results highlight the complementary roles of visual and metric evidence in agent-based Scan-to-BIM.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) provides high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis, but deployment budgets vary across devices and network conditions. Most compact methods target one operating point, so another budget requires a separate representation. SHIN is a scalable 3DGS hierarchy with a reusable Base and additive enhancement layers, so each higher-quality operating point extends rather than replaces the decoded representation. Constructing this hierarchy requires deciding which Gaussians each stage adds and how much spherical-harmonic (SH) appearance each retains, because these choices remain fixed in later prefixes. SHIN derives one shared Gaussian order from multi-scale position-scale sensitivity and divides each stage budget between adding Gaussians and retaining SH bands using calibrated gradient-based scores per byte. Lightweight opacity updates adjust compositing while transmitted geometry and SH coefficients remain unchanged. Across three benchmarks, SHIN progressively improves one cumulative representation and achieves the best PSNR-size efficiency in every overlapping comparison with the multi-rate and progressive baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dense prediction and generative modeling in resource-constrained scenarios remain challenging, as repeated downsampling substantially reduces spatial detail and degrades boundaries. Although recent attention mechanisms have alleviated the problem of semantic ambiguity, they often fail to preserve high-frequency features or do so at the expense of a very high computational cost. In this paper, we propose the Frequency Guided Depthwise Separable Attention for Efficient Dense Prediction (FG-DSA), a novel framework for efficient dense visual understanding and generation. FG-DSA employs a 2D FFT high-pass filtering module to explicitly inject frequency domain priors into the spatial attention mechanism, allowing lightweight networks to recover sharp boundary transitions with linear complexity. A range of experiments show that FG-DSA is effective at recovering fine object boundaries and leads to a consistent performance gain in the task of semantic segmentation. Importantly, we show that FGA-DSA can be used as a module in diffusion-based generative U-Nets, leading to a significant improvement in the detail of the generated images with very little additional parameter and Floating Point Operation (FLOP) cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Latent world models enable visual control by planning action sequences in compact representation spaces, but a gap remains between how latent dynamics are learned and how they are used for planning. First, teacher-forced training does not expose the dynamics model to the recursively predicted latent states encountered during planning. Second, sampling-based planners typically initialize action search from goal-agnostic proposals, without exploiting the action-conditioned dynamics learned by the world model. We introduce Dynamics-Informed Action Learning (DIAL) to bridge these two gaps. During training, rollout alignment exposes the forward model to its own autoregressive latent predictions, while multi-step inverse supervision trains a lightweight inverse dynamics head to infer actions from latent-state transitions. We further apply the inverse head to model-generated transitions with its parameters held fixed, encouraging predicted rollouts to retain action-relevant information. At test time, DIAL autoregressively couples the inverse and forward dynamics to construct a dynamics-informed action sequence toward the goal, which initializes the mean of the Cross-Entropy Method (CEM) proposal distribution. The subsequent CEM search then refines this initialization using the standard sampling and updating procedure. Experiments across four visual control benchmarks demonstrate that DIAL outperforms existing latent world models and planning baselines in both standard and long-horizon scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Low-light conditions reduce visible contrast and obscure details, while infrared images lack color and texture, motivating the fusion of infrared and visible information for visual tasks. Existing fusion methods primarily optimize image appearance or task-specific features without explicitly disentangling the coupled factors underlying thermal observations, limiting their physical interpretation under thermal degeneracy. Hyperspectral thermal measurements enable physical decomposition, but they require specialized imaging hardware, while pixel-level physical annotations for supervision or evaluation incur additional costs. We propose TeX-Gen, a physically guided framework for TeX generation from paired visible and infrared images in low-light visual tasks. TeX Generation constructs complementary multi-view features and employs independent branch-specific gates to route cues into the Temperature, Emissivity, and Texture maps. TeXMetric integrates radiometric reconstruction, channel structure, and material consistency to assess the generated maps without requiring pixel-level physical labels. TeX Tune improves performance through task-guided adjustments, jointly optimizing the adjustment modules and task network while keeping the generator frozen. Experiments show that TeX-Gen achieves competitive task performance in low-light environments on the FLIR, LLVIP, M3FD, and MSRS datasets. Experiments on HADAR also examine the association between TeXMetric scores and downstream detection performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Knowledge distillation (KD) between architectural families, e.g., from a Transformer teacher to a CNN student, is difficult because the two networks learn different representations. Logits share a label space, but heterogeneous teachers differ in logit offset, scale, and sharpness, and their non-target classes are less semantically organized. Conventional logit distillation matches these values sample by sample, asking the student to imitate how another architecture expresses its predictions. We argue that a heterogeneous teacher should pass on the relational structure of its predictions, i.e., how it arranges samples relative to one another, and propose Heterogeneous Asymmetric Contrastive Distillation (HACD) to transfer this structure. HACD maps teacher and student logits to centered, temperature-softened probability directions on a shared unit hypersphere, which removes architecture-dependent offsets and lets the temperature control how sharply peaked outputs enter the representation while keeping informative non-target classes. Each student output must then identify its own teacher target within the batch. Because every teacher target also acts as a negative for the others, HACD computes the targets on a moderately augmented view and contrasts both a moderate and a strong student view against them. HACD adds no learnable parameters and needs one teacher pass per sample. It achieves the best or second-best accuracy on 11 of 12 heterogeneous pairs on CIFAR-100, without ground-truth labels, and on 14 of 15 pairs on ImageNet-1K, improving over the reported vanilla KD results by 8.82 and 1.33 percentage points on average. Checkpoints are available at https://github.com/BTAEYANG/HACD-Heterogeneous-Asymmetric-Contrastive-Distillation; the code will be released upon publication.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recently, diffusion models have achieved remarkable performance in image generation. However, their inherent memory properties introduce a potential safety risk of training data leakage, i.e., data extraction attack. To illustrate such risk, we propose a membership guided extraction (MGE) framework to effectively extract training data from a pre-trained diffusion model. Our core idea is to formulate the data extraction as a conditional generation, where the condition signal is from a membership inference classifier. To improve the success rate and efficiency of MGE, we propose adversarial gradient and parallel branch decoding. Our experiments on image generation benchmarks demonstrate that our MGE can extract hundreds of images, which shows that more attention should be paid to privacy-preserving of diffusion model.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generating high-fidelity voxels while keeping their lightweight nature remains an open problem in computer vision. Current generators are either high-fidelity but computationally heavy, or lightweight but of lower fidelity. Hierarchical methods keep the lightweight nature of voxels. However, their coarse levels, which decide where all finer cells can appear, are generated with the least information about the shape and are never revisited, so they are often plausible but wrong. Our key insight is to let finer cells take part in deciding coarse cells. To this end, we propose JoVox, a discrete flow matching framework that learns the joint distribution of all levels of the voxel hierarchy, instead of a separate distribution for each level, so that coarse cells can be predicted with finer cells already generated nearby. Within this framework, we further introduce a two-level prediction, which outputs the cells two levels finer at once instead of one level finer, halving the number of levels that the network processes and yielding voxels of higher fidelity. We also introduce an unmasking strategy that revisits early decisions once finer cells are generated and corrects inconsistencies in masked diffusion models. Our framework outperforms the baselines on both unconditional and image-conditioned voxel generation. Compared with the current state-of-the-art TRELLIS.2, we achieve 8x higher training throughput, 1/15 of the FLOPs in a forward pass, train on 4 vs. 32 GPUs, and obtain higher quality with an 11% lower Chamfer distance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models pass text-based false-belief probes at near-human levels but drop by over twenty percentage points on abstract panels the moment the same problem is conveyed visually. The consensus diagnosis is conceptual, prescribing new belief-reasoning modules. We argue the dominant bottleneck is perceptual: not what the model cannot reason about, but what it cannot see. We propose a five-condition dissociation protocol that holds the reasoning task constant while varying perceptual fidelity from visual only to text only, and quantify the result with the Perceptual Contribution Ratio, the share of the visual-to-text gap a given fidelity closes. We test this on two kinds of scenes: abstract 2D panels in the Heider--Simmel tradition, and naturalistic 3D game scenes from MuMA-ToM and MindPower. The evaluation covers seven VLMs and roughly 159,000 API calls. Ground-truth scaffolds substantially close the gap in every setting, and on the abstract panels and MuMA-ToM they match or exceed the text-only reasoning. Whereas scaffolds built by automatic parsers, including frontier VLMs, fail. The most common error in the visual-only condition is \emph{reality bias}, which accounts for 60\% of false-belief failures. Models report where an object actually is, not where an absent agent believes it is. This points to a perceptual front-end that fails to track which agents were present at each moment. Under the evaluated protocol, structured visual scene-state extraction, rather than belief inference, is the dominant bottleneck for visual ToM.

</details>

Abstract (英文): While 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is typically optimized from images only, many robotics and mapping applications provide both camera and high-definition LiDAR data, combining rich visual appearance with accurate geometric structure. We introduce PLS-GS, a probabilistic LiDAR scaffold for Gaussian Splatting that exploits this complementary sensing setting to construct a compact 3DGS representation aligned to the measured surface. PLS-GS fits a Hierarchical Gaussian Mixture Model jointly over position and color, yielding a continuous and adaptive scaffold that eliminates the need for discrete scene partitioning such as voxel grids or octrees. The fitted mixture then guides the renderable 3DGS Gaussians: each rendering Gaussian is associated with a GMM component and constrained in the local surface frame induced by that component's covariance. A Mahalanobis support loss bounds the deviation of the rendering Gaussians from the corresponding LiDAR-derived surface. Across five large outdoor scenes, PLS-GS places primitives markedly closer to the LiDAR measurements than both image- and LiDAR-initialized baselines, while employing substantially fewer primitives, yielding a considerably more compact model and faster rendering. At matched capacity, PLS-GS places primitives closer to the LiDAR measurements, with comparable rendering quality. Code is available at https://github.com/k-harikumar/PLS-GS.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Stroke-based rendering is often optimized as a pixel-level reconstruction task. This over-reliance on global error reduction not only degrades strokes into fragmented curves due to a lack of structured planning, but also allows locally defective primitives to be concealed by overlapping layers. To address these issues, we introduce a model-guided framework for stroke-audited rendering. Our framework uses Quadratic Bezier Splatting to analytically model continuous curved strokes with efficient differentiable alpha compositing. Built on this renderer, a feedforward multi-scale stroke decoder provides a structural initialization with learned stroke-scale allocation, while test-time stroke optimization supplies the reconstruction capacity needed for high-fidelity refinement. We further propose a single-stroke audit mechanism that explicitly evaluates local stroke quality, preventing defective primitives from surviving optimization through layer-wise compensation. Compared with existing methods, our approach achieves coherent layouts, improves PSNR by up to 5.46 dB, yields a 16% more compact representation, and accelerates combined renderer forward and backward computation by a factor of 220.3.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image-text alignment models such as CLIP are typically trained with contrastive learning, where unpaired examples are treated as strict negatives. While this assumption introduces noise by penalising semantically related pairs, existing solutions often rely on heuristic similarity thresholds that lack statistical grounding and sensitivity to dataset-specific noise distributions. In this paper, we propose Permute-then-Adapt (PTA), a framework that leverages efficient weak models to identify missed positives while controlling Type-I error relative to a conditional permutation reference, up to calibration approximation error. Unlike standard distillation or arbitrary thresholding, PTA estimates a random-pairing reference distribution from the weak teacher's similarity scores and uses its upper tail, determined by $α$, to select auxiliary positives. The calibrated positive set is then trained against by a single multi-positive contrastive objective: each anchor maximises the total probability mass it assigns to its positive set. Offline calibration with the weak model adds negligible training overhead. Experiments show gains over heuristic soft-label approaches in object recognition, cross-modal retrieval, and data efficiency. Project repository: https://github.com/JinhaoLee/Permute-then-Adapt.

</details>

Abstract (英文): With a solid theoretical foundation and pretraining on large-scale datasets, diffusion-based methods have achieved controllable generation leveraging text and image conditions, and generating excellent stylized images is possible using diffusion models. However, previous methods face significant challenges. First, because diffusion generation is iterative, the common approach of learning implicit style representations via cycle-consistency strategies is too costly and not well-suited to diffusion, even for LoRA-based methods. Second, decoupling style is difficult in the latent space of diffusion models. Representations of attributes are coupled in the latent space of diffusion models; thus, extracting style attributes with diffusion models usually suffers from the issue of content leakage. Furthermore, because style is hard to represent in natural language, capturing style using text prompts or textual inversion is deficient. In this paper, we propose a method for achieving disentangled stylized image generation through IP-cross-attention rearrangement, based on our experimental observations. We also propose negative content guidance, inspired by classifier-free guidance, to reduce content leakage in stylized image generation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are increasingly adopted as end-to-end driving planners, yet they suffer from cross-city domain shift. Existing methods treat this shift as a single quantity addressed by uniform alignment or single-site editing. We challenge this view on two counts: (i) domain shift in VLM trajectory planners is inherently structured across modality and layer, and (ii) a locate-then-repair recipe matched to this structure outperforms every uniform-objective baseline. We analyse domain shift via activation patching and uncover two regularities. (F1) Modality axis. Image and text tokens exhibit distinct shift patterns in each VLM. (F2) Layer axis. The layer-wise shift partition is architecture-dependent and dataset-stable. These findings recast domain shift as a two-dimensional tensor indexed by modality (image vs. text tokens) and layer, motivating a simple principle: locate where shift occurs, and repair only there. We instantiate this principle in MoLaRx (Modality-Layer Repair), a locate-then-repair framework. The Causal Importance Score (CIS) estimates the domain shift as a modality-layer tensor and partitions the layers into functional zones. CIS-Guided Selective Repair (CISR) then applies a matched operator (Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) / low-rank adaptation (LoRA) / freezing) to repair each zone, with the composition adapting automatically to each architecture. We evaluate MoLaRx on three architecturally distinct VLMs (LFM2-VL-1.6B, Qwen2-VL-2B, and PaliGemma2-3B) across two cross-city benchmarks (nuScenes and Argoverse 2). MoLaRx attains the lowest cross-city trajectory-error gap among adaptation methods in all six main-table settings, while using 50-60% fewer trainable parameters and 8.4-10.9 ms lower inference latency. In contrast, uniform MMD-only adaptation collapses in all six settings, highlighting the importance of structure-guided repair.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Few-shot depth completion aims to recover dense metric depth from sparse measurements using only a small number of target-domain annotations, offering a favorable balance between annotation cost and model adaptation. We propose LIFT, a framework that harnesses both depth and feature priors from a frozen pretrained monocular depth estimation (MDE) foundation model for few-shot depth completion. Specifically, LIFT first estimates local scale and shift parameters on a coarse grid and interpolates them into smooth, dense parameter fields, providing a spatially adaptive metric depth initialization. It then progressively refines this initialization through multi-scale depth propagation guided by foundation features. Finally, a lightweight residual decoder combines multi-level foundation features with propagation correction to refine the metric depth. Extensive experiments on NYUv2 and KITTI-DC demonstrate that our proposed method achieves state-of-the-art few-shot performance. The source code will soon be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models have made progress in general visual tasks. However, limitations remain in fine-grained visual tasks requiring evidence from minute image regions. Existing methods primarily train models using evidence-related regions while overlooking the influence of extensive background regions, resulting in a limited ability to resist interference from irrelevant regions. We propose selective positive-negative on-policy self-distillation (SPN-OPSD), which considers both evidence and background regions. Supervision from both views improves the model's ability to resist interference from irrelevant regions. Furthermore, selective token-level distillation provides more informative supervision, improving the model's ability to attend to evidence regions in Fine-Grained Visual Tasks. On Qwen3.5-2B, SPN-OPSD improves accuracy on V* Bench, ZoomBench, and MME-RealWorld-Lite from 76.44%, 45.92%, and 57.43% to 84.82%, 54.56%, and 59.56%, respectively. Similar gains are also observed on Qwen3.5-0.8B.

Abstract (英文): Handedness is a soft-biometric cue for forensic document examination and writer profiling, yet reported accuracies from offline handwriting rarely keep writers, texts and image acquisition apart, so models may recognise the writer or capture conditions rather than the writing hand. Such controlled evidence is missing for Bangla. Using our BanglaHand dataset of 42 writers (21 left- and 21 right-handed), who each wrote six shared prompts and two own texts under controlled collection and photography, we evaluate handedness identification with all images of each test writer held out, whole-writer permutation tests and photometric controls. An ink-gradient descriptor identified the handedness of unseen writers with 77.4% balanced accuracy (95% writer-bootstrap interval 67.7-86.5%; p=0.001) and frozen ImageNet embeddings with 69.5-72.0%. Part of this signal is stroke darkness: in a post hoc analysis, right-handed writers' strokes were darker at their centres for equal stroke width, consistent with higher pen pressure and not explained by lighting drift. With ink density and brightness regressed out, the descriptor still reached 64.5% (p=0.027), whereas writing-only shape features were weaker (60.8%; p=0.090). Handedness is thus identifiable from offline Bangla handwriting under controlled acquisition, and photometric controls are essential to interpret such cues.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Lane-level map updating requires temporally consistent, physically valid, and auditable road-scene semantics despite occlusions, ambiguous lane markings, temporary obstacles, and viewpoint changes. We present RoadState, a production-deployed vision-language system that transforms sequential road-scene observations into structured lane-level semantics for Baidu Maps' map-updating pipeline. Its core model, RoadState-VLM, formulates road-scene understanding as autoregressive state estimation, predicting the current structured road state from the previous observation and symbolic state together with the current observation. We introduce Geometric Chain-of-Thought (Geo-CoT), which uses a directed acyclic graph of semantic dependencies as a domain prior to improve semantic consistency and reasoning fidelity across interdependent road-state dimensions. Extensive experiments on a real-world dataset show that RoadState-VLM achieves 94.33% mean accuracy across six road-state dimensions, outperforming the strongest fine-tuned VLM baseline. Ablation studies further demonstrate the complementary effectiveness of Geo-CoT and temporal transition context. A cascaded routing pipeline directs routine cases to lightweight models while reserving complex cases for RoadState-VLM. Since its deployment in March 2025, RoadState has supported lane-level map updating across more than 360 cities and reduced overall production costs by 30%. Together with human-in-the-loop auditing, the system maintains robust performance under stress-test conditions. These results demonstrate how domain-prior-enhanced reasoning, structured state interfaces, selective routing, and human quality control can bridge the gap between VLM-based road-scene understanding and large-scale lane-level map updating.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Skeleton-based rehabilitation assessment faces fundamental limitations of fixed topologies which cannot model compensatory patterns, uniform joint treatments overlook clinically important deviations, and lack of geometric understand- ing. We propose Concave-Convex Decomposition Sparse Topological Graph Convolutional Network (CC2-STG), a multi-stream model that combines geo- metric decomposition, sparse feature learning, and adaptive topology. Our framework decomposes joint relationships into complementary convex-concave distance functions to model both local and global movement characteristics, with sparse regularization with proximal optimization selecting clinically relevant geo- metric features, while multi-scale topological analysis captures non-anatomical joint dependencies in pathological movements. Experiments on the KiMoRe and UIPRMD datasets validate the effectiveness of our approach, achieving state-of-the-art performance on clinical pathological movement assessment.

Abstract (英文): Medical foundation models (Med-FMs) are increasingly adapted to clinical tasks through prompt learning, which tunes a small set of prompt tokens while the backbone remains frozen. Recent work has shown that this efficiency introduces a vulnerability: jointly optimizing learnable prompts and an imperceptible noise trigger during few-shot adaptation implants a backdoor that leaves clean performance intact but steers triggered inputs to an attacker-chosen class. Since prompts are compact and readily shared, this raises two questions: whether a backdoored prompt remains effective on a different Med-FM, and whether it can be repaired without retraining the backbone. We address both in BAPLe++. First, we present the first study of cross-model transferability of prompt-based backdoors, quantified by two proposed metrics, Clean Accuracy Difference (CAD) and Backdoor Accuracy Difference (BAD). Second, we propose DePLe (Defense via Prompt Learning), which uses the infected prompt as a fixed anchor and learns a new prompt on a small clean set that preserves the clean task while diverging from the compromised embedding region. Experiments on four Med-FMs and six chest X-ray and histopathology datasets show that backdoors transfer across models, but asymmetrically and in a dataset-dependent manner. Compared with four training-free input transformations, DePLe reduces backdoor accuracy more consistently while preserving, and in several settings improving, clean accuracy. Our code can be accessed on https://github.com/Ufaqkhan/Baple-Plus-Plus

</details>
