Abstract (英文): Foundation models increasingly combine geometry, semantics, and spatial context for three-dimensional scene understanding, but their suitability for forensic crime scene reconstruction remains unclear. This study presents a structured forensic evaluation framework for egocentric foundation models and demonstrates it using the Egocentric Foundation Model for 3D (EFM3D). Four simulated indoor crime scenes were recorded with Meta Project Aria Gen~1 at Robert Gordon University's East Lodge House forensic training facility. The framework evaluates room-scale reconstruction, semantic scene understanding, computational performance, and forensic suitability. Across the four scenes, EFM3D generated meshes averaging $12{,}668\pm2{,}430$ vertices and $24{,}838\pm4{,}903$ faces, with 39,529 recorded semantic predictions spanning 21--24 categories at a mean confidence of 0.40. Because reference meshes and expert-labelled 3D ground truth were unavailable, these statistics are treated as descriptive outputs rather than measures of geometric or detection accuracy. Final inference runs processed $1{,}388\pm203$ snippets per scene at $0.69\pm0.01$ samples/s and required $50.77\pm6.12$ minutes per recording. A seven-criterion capability profile indicated stronger support for room geometry, furniture localisation, scene context, and spatial relationships than for fine-scale forensic evidence. The results position EFM3D as a complementary tool for contextual documentation and scene review rather than a replacement for high-fidelity forensic capture. The framework provides a reproducible basis for evaluating future egocentric foundation models in specialised forensic settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated strong performance on knowledge-intensive and reasoning-based vision-language benchmark datasets, but still struggle to solve the visual questions that people can solve easily. Evaluating the differences between MLLMs and human-level visual perception is challenging due to end-to-end evaluation which combines various skills, or because we are uncertain if individual items are valid. Specifically, observed errors may reflect visual perception, language priors, background knowledge, question ambiguity, and underspecified answers. Our guideline for HumanSightBench is that we construct items that are human-easy by construction and model-hard by evaluation. HumanSightBench contains 2,876 visual questions with short answers across 7 top-level perceptual categories and 21 subcategories. We only include items that pass five inclusion gates: question clarity, answer uniqueness, visual grounding, human solvability, and text-only shortcut filtering. According to the protocol, errors are defined as incorrect answers on human-validated visual tasks with the evaluated interface. The inclusion gates are intended to reduce confounds from ambiguity and external knowledge. The best pass@3 score is 65.6% across eight evaluated MLLMs. No evaluated model exceeds 27.5% on Counting. These results identify capability gaps that are hidden by overall scores and can help future training experiments and held-out evaluation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Discrete autoregressive modeling, notably through next-token prediction, has become a cornerstone of modern language models. Extending this paradigm to vision allows visual and language models to converge on a unified framework, thereby fostering shared methodology and infrastructure. This work investigates a central question: how far can a finite discrete vocabulary go in representing the richness of the visual world? We present dNaViT, a Discrete Native-resolution Vision Transformer that produces discrete visual primitives analogous to language tokens. Built upon a frozen pretrained encoder, dNaViT applies lightweight residual modulation with a dual-branch objective to inject reconstruction-aware cues into semantic features. By leveraging grouped product quantization, it scales discrete capacity without inflating spatial sequence lengths, while a balanced training recipe maintains both semantic and pixel-level information. Consequently, dNaViT functions as a versatile discrete visual tokenizer, enabling unified encoding across downstream tasks alongside high-fidelity image decoding. Extensive evaluations show that dNaViT matches or exceeds task-specific baselines across 12 visual understanding benchmarks, 4 generation benchmarks, and an image editing benchmark. These findings demonstrate that a compact discrete vocabulary can serve as a universal visual currency and unlock the full potential of fully unified, native multimodal methodology.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models such as CLIP achieve strong performance across computer vision tasks, yet their image-text alignment is less reliable for infrared (IR) imagery because large-scale pre-training is dominated by visible-spectrum data. Due to the high cost of the infrared-text labeling, we investigate adapting CLIP to IR through only paired infrared-visible images, which requires considering modality-specific knowledge while preserving scene content and CLIP's image-text alignment. To achieve this, we propose TMT (Text-Derived Modality Knowledge Transfer), which adapts CLIP's image encoder through a text-defined infrared-to-visible direction and supervision from paired IR-visible images. First, we found that modality discrepancy can be described by corresponding text embeddings, thus using the direction of text embedding differences to guide the knowledge transfer between visible and infrared modalities without erasing infrared-specific information. To enhance the representation of semantics and details of infrared images, we introduce Generative-Perceptual Co-Supervision that combines semantic alignment from CLIP visible image encoder with diffusion noise-prediction feedbacks. On four source-disjoint IR classification benchmarks, TMT achieves the highest mean zero-shot accuracy among the compared methods (48.92%). It also achieves the best IR-text retrieval results among the compared methods without infrared-text supervision during adaptation and the highest open-vocabulary segmentation mIoU on both CART and SemanticRT among the CLIP-like methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generalizing deepfake detection across facial manipulation and fully generated video remains challenging due to forgery traces varying with the manipulation method and scene content. Explicit spatial and temporal feature differences offer a way to capture this variation, but also respond to natural texture and motion, motivating their joint interpretation with appearance features. We investigate whether combining multiscale feature differences with adapted appearance representations improves detection across both forgery categories. We introduce Multiscale Appearance-Residual Streams (MARS), a two-path video detector. The residual path applies fixed spatial and temporal difference-of-Gaussians operators to frozen intermediate features and models the resulting differences alongside appearance context across frames. The semantic path adapts the encoder's normalization parameters for frame classification, and a clip-dependent gate combines their predictions. Under a shared protocol across seven datasets, MARS achieves the highest average AUROC of 91.43\%, outperforming seven compared detectors. When trained on facial manipulations alone, MARS achieves 93.99\% AUROC on GenVideo and 75.50\% on DF26, demonstrating transfer to generated videos without generated-video training data. MARS also provides signed patch and frame contributions, whose faithfulness we evaluate through controlled spatial and temporal removal against random controls. MARS is publicly available for research: Project page: https://vectorinstitute.github.io/mars/ Github: https://github.com/VectorInstitute/mars

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large-scale annotation of vectorized high-definition (HD) maps is labor-intensive and costly, motivating local-to-global auto-labeling approaches that incrementally construct global maps from local vectorized predictions. However, existing approaches often overlook prediction uncertainty and rely on heuristic map integration, potentially propagating errors and introducing topological inconsistencies. To address these limitations, we propose ReVMap, a reliable local-to-global framework that iteratively refines local map predictions and global map construction through uncertainty-guided prior utilization and connectivity-guided global map revision. Specifically, ReVMap leverages point-wise localization uncertainty to incorporate global map priors into local map prediction, improving robustness against inaccurate prior information. It then predicts local-to-global connectivity and selectively updates the global map through uncertainty-aware fusion based on the reliability of local predictions. By iteratively coupling local prediction and global revision, ReVMap progressively improves both local map accuracy and global map consistency. Extensive experiments on the nuScenes and Argoverse 2 benchmarks demonstrate that ReVMap achieves state-of-the-art performance in both local map prediction and global map construction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video world models conditioned on an input video make it possible to explore real, recorded dynamic scenes, enabling re-shooting of footage, free-viewpoint replay, and real-to-sim environments for embodied agents. Such uses require the generated rollout to stay faithful to the recorded world, yet existing benchmarks start from a single image or a text prompt, and video-conditioned generation is evaluated mainly on perceptual quality and camera following. These evaluations do not directly assess whether exploration preserves subject identity, event progression, and scene consistency upon revisiting a location. We introduce WanderBench, a benchmark for exploring dynamic worlds from video. It comprises 745 single-shot source videos of real and AI-generated content in three difficulty tiers (3-25 s), paired with camera routes given as keyboard actions, and evaluates six dimensions with 24 human-validated sub-metrics. Benchmarking eight models, we find that as clips grow longer, models keep the static scene and visual quality but lose camera control and the source dynamics, and that none keeps revisited places consistent. We further propose WanderLand, a training-free framework that conditions each frame of a frozen model on the same-instant source and, on revisits, on its own earlier static content. WanderLand improves five models on every dimension, by up to 8.3 points in source-dynamics fidelity.

Abstract (英文): A key challenge in action-conditioned world modeling is to construct macro-states from high-dimensional observations that support recursive prediction under actions, while keeping their predicted evolution consistent with visual changes in generated observations. We propose QCC-WM, a visual world model based on Quotient-based Causal Construction. QCC-WM uses action responses to guide macro-variable construction for recursive action-conditioned prediction. A shared reference quotient measurement constrains the consistency between predicted states and observed changes, allowing macro-level predictions to provide structured constraints for future visual generation.
  Using translational and rotational actions and their predicted responses, the model aggregates local visual features into macro-nodes with spatial correspondence through causal abstraction, and recursively predicts node content and motion under a shared reference measurement. Macro-level predictions guide future observation generation, while predicted motion transports historical appearance information. Node-level errors between generated observations and macro-level targets are then mapped back to the corresponding spatial regions for correction, improving consistency between visual generation and macro-level predictions.
  On the US-VLA dataset, QCC-WM achieves an ROI-MSE of 0.01861 for four-step prediction. Compared with the best baseline at each prediction horizon, it reduces LPIPS by 48.59%, 41.68%, and 30.23% for four-step prediction and eight- and sixteen-step recursive extrapolation, respectively. Ablation studies and mechanism analyses show that motion-guided reference transport mainly improves pixel reconstruction, while node-level error feedback further improves perceptual quality and consistency between generated observations and macro-level predictions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing images, the objects that questions count in a region or relate by direction often span only a few pixels. Existing methods mainly decide where a vision-language model (VLM) should look and trust its answer in the chosen view. We show that this assumption fails for counting, and measure VLMs as one calibrates a sensor. All eight open VLMs tested fall increasingly short once a view holds more than a certain number of objects, about five for most on DOTA aerial images. This counting horizon narrows as views grow and objects appear smaller. An empirical power-law response model predicts the undercount a split recovers and the spurious counts it adds, and supports the rule of splitting only views beyond the horizon. In GeoScope, a text-only language model parses the question into a typed spatial program that fixes the region and its resolution. The VLM only grounds and counts within its operating range, and directions are computed explicitly from the grounded boxes. To our knowledge, it is the first UHR method to split views by a measured limit of the VLM. Without training, it improves Qwen3-VL-8B by 16.3 points on regional counting and 10.5 on spatial relations in XLRS-Bench, and has the highest reported accuracy, trained methods included, on regional counting and on the whole benchmark by question. On UHR-Micro it improves global and regional counting by 16.8 and 36.2 points, 15.6 of the former from splitting. On MME-RealWorld counting it gains 11.4 points, and we show that an option that is almost never correct yet often chosen distorts comparisons there. Project page: https://avalon-s.github.io/GeoScope/

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal retrieval increasingly supports agentic systems, where user requests can involve cross-level relations, combine multiple constraints, and evolve across dialogue turns. In these settings, a highly similar result may still violate the user's requirements, while retrieved content may directly inform downstream actions without human screening. We introduce TRIAD, a multimodal retrieval benchmark for evaluating how well retrieval systems satisfy these complex, evolving requests. TRIAD comprises three tracks: abstraction, testing bidirectional retrieval between visual instances and concepts at different hierarchical levels; constraints, testing the joint satisfaction of positive and negative conditions; and state, testing the integration of requirements across dialogue turns and the updating of earlier conditions when users revise their requests. The benchmark contains six subsets, each with 1,000 queries and a fixed pool of 1,000 candidates per query, evaluated using a common multi-positive mean average precision (mAP) protocol. Simpler reference tasks and hard negatives grouped by the requirements they violate help characterize specific retrieval difficulties. We evaluate 20 configurations of 17 systems and find that no system leads across all six subsets. Performance declines with hierarchical distance, and negative-only queries generally underperform positive-only queries with the same number of constraints. Across paired tasks expressing the same final intent, multi-turn retrieval yields lower mAP in 39 of 40 configuration-domain comparisons. The benefits of explicit reasoning also vary across models and tasks. These findings highlight the gap between retrieving similar content and satisfying a complete request.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dense non-rigid registration, the recovery of a pixel-wise deformation field that warps one image into geometric agreement with another, is fundamental across medical imaging, structural biology, and correspondence estimation, yet learning-based approaches face a severe bottleneck: ground-truth deformation fields are notoriously difficult to obtain for real data. We present 2D Gum-Net, an end-to-end architecture that predicts dense elastic deformation fields fully unsupervised. We transfer the Geometric Unsupervised Matching Network (Gum-Net), originally developed for 3D subtomogram alignment in cryo-electron tomography, to 2D elastic registration: all three modules are redesigned in 2D, and the rigid transformation head is replaced by a Dense Spatial Transformer that regresses a sparse control-point grid, lifted to a full-resolution field $Φ$ by bicubic interpolation. Downsampling uses DCT spectral pooling, implemented as frozen-basis matrix multiplications ($\approx 50\times$ GPU speedup), preserving the fine, quasi-periodic structure that max and average pooling destroy. We evaluate on contact-based fingerprints, a challenging testbed for this task: locally self-similar ridge patterns make correspondence maximally ambiguous, skin pressed against a sensor generates authentic large-displacement elastic distortion, and minutiae act as precise landmarks through which an independent matcher externally verifies the topological correctness of predicted fields. Trained purely on synthetic impressions and evaluated zero-shot on real benchmarks, our method improves structural correlation by up to 15.7% on FVC2004 and raises genuine match scores under an independent matcher while leaving imposter scores essentially unchanged. This suggests that the predicted deformations restore true structure without fabricating correspondences.

</details>

Abstract (英文): An edge video analytics system must estimate the quality of local detections since there are no ground-truth labels available during runtime. Detectors usually provide confidence scores for their results, but these scores can not reveal every missed object or misplaced bounding box. So a quality estimator is necessary to provide a decision signal indicating which frame has potential detection failures that need to be escalated to a more powerful model in the cloud. Neural network based estimators add workload to the GPU already running detection, while CPU capacity may remain available. We present Predictive Temporal Consistency IoU (PTC-IoU), which moves this workload to CPU by compacting detection boxes histories to support deterministic result scoring. Comparing to neural counterparts, experiments on representative edge hardware, Nvidia Jetson Orin Nano, show that our method reduced per-frame estimator latency from 10.3 ms for neural counterparts to 2.2 ms (4.7x faster), increased pipelined throughput by 13.6%, and cuts estimator energy consumption by up to 95%, while maintaining competitive final detection quality verified on multiple datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): While modern video generative models achieve remarkable visual fidelity, they remain noticeably brittle when generating physically plausible dynamics and complex interactions. Targeted post-training is crucial, yet supervised fine-tuning suffers from offline state mismatch, and reinforcement learning is hindered by sparse, ill-defined physical reward functions. On-Policy (Self-)Distillation (OPSD) reconciles on-policy rollouts with dense corrective feedback, shifting the core challenge to designing effective privileged context. However, conventional modalities fall short: text prompts are inherently declarative, whereas real reference videos heavily entangle physical laws with visual appearance without parametric control. To this end, we propose PhysOPSD, introducing executable code as privileged physical context for video generation. Unlike static visual observations, executable code instantiates an editable physical world with intrinsic abstraction and controllability. Powered by PhysOPSD-Engine, we translate real-world physical scenarios into verifiable simulation programs. PhysOPSD then incorporates (i) dual-level physical privilege to bridge the information gap via an intermediate bottleneck, alongside (ii) controlled physical variations under shared rollout noise to expose causal dynamic shifts. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PhysOPSD substantially improves physical plausibility across diverse backbones, outperforming SFT and RL baselines with only $3$K samples, while preserving general video generation quality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Landslide susceptibility models are trained on inventories that record where landslides were seen, not where they occurred. The established remedy fits bias-describing variables and neutralises them when mapping. We adapt occupancy-detection modelling from ecology, treating several inventories as imperfect observers of one event and separating occurrence from recording, and ask when this improves on neutralisation. We use near-complete inventories of the 2018 Hokkaido Eastern Iburi, 2015 Gorkha and 2010 Haiti earthquakes with simulated reporting bias, and three real Gorkha inventories. Compared with standard practice, the occupancy model reduces road bias in 24 of 25 paired draws and raises AUC by 0.08-0.14 on Gorkha and 0.03 on Haiti, but only 0.01-0.02 on Iburi. Its median predicted-to-true landslide rate is 0.99 (range 0.44-1.89), against 0.24-0.30 for the baselines. Rescaling the baselines with the same anchor area corrects their median but leaves larger errors (range 0.20-4.34). Cell-level probabilities remain too extreme (calibration slope 0.59). Compared with the best neutralised model, AUC is higher on Iburi (by 0.03-0.07) and lower on Gorkha and Haiti (by 0.01-0.05). Transplant experiments tie the Iburi advantage to the structure of its true landslide pattern, not to its terrain or road network. In all eight settings with a low interaction gap, computable from biased records, neutralisation was at least as accurate, including a prespecified test on Haiti; a high gap did not guarantee an advantage. The real Gorkha inventories are slope-biased, not road-biased, and there the model brings no meaningful gain. Report-based catalogues remain the main untested case.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generalizable deepfake video detection remains challenging because forgery artifacts vary substantially across manipulation types and often combine diverse spatial and temporal inconsistencies. Existing parameter-efficient adaptation methods typically rely on a single shared representation, rendering them prone to manipulation-specific bias and limiting transfer to unseen forgeries. To address this challenge, we propose Mixture Of Spatio-Temporal Experts (MoSTE), an adaptation framework that enables conditional specialization of forensic representations. Instead of forcing one adapter to model all forgery patterns, MoSTE decomposes adaptation into parallel spatial and temporal expert pools and dynamically routes tokens to the most relevant experts through an unsupervised token-level routing mechanism. This allows experts to naturally specialize in complementary forgery cues without requiring explicit manipulation labels or multi-stage training, while effectively transferring representations from CLIP to the video domain. Extensive experiments across cross-dataset and cross-manipulation benchmarks demonstrate that MoSTE sets a new state of the art in average generalization while preserving strong performance across diverse manipulation families. Additional analysis of expert routing and attention behavior reveals the emergence of expert diversity, suggesting that MoSTE learns complementary spatio-temporal forensic features. Code is available at https://github.com/charbelyah/MoSTE

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video out-painting is an important problem, yet existing research focuses primarily on short clips, leaving long video out-painting largely underexplored. Extending video out-painting to long videos poses substantial challenges. A natural solution is iterative out-painting, i.e., generating videos chunk by chunk, but this introduces two major issues: generation instability or even failure caused by inter-chunk inconsistencies and error accumulation, and semantic drift over long temporal horizons. In this paper, we propose LongSCOP, short for Semantically Consistent long Video Out-Painting. First, we introduce a training recipe with three components for smooth and stable long-horizon generation: self-forcing training, latent calibration, and future references. Beyond generation stability, we recognize that the rich temporal context of long videos provides valuable references for maintaining semantic consistency. We therefore develop a VLM-based agent that automatically selects the most informative reference frames from the entire video to maintain consistent subjects, scenes, and backgrounds throughout long-horizon generation. We also construct a high-quality training dataset of 62K video clips. To facilitate research on this underexplored problem, we introduce LVO-Bench, a benchmark curated by human experts to assess long-horizon stability and semantic consistency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms existing approaches in both generation stability and semantic coherence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-view anomaly detection is crucial for industrial inspection to identify defects hidden from single viewpoints. Yet, despite their strong success in single-view applications, reconstruction-based methods remain largely underexplored in multi-view settings. A key challenge in extending these methods lies in generating view-consistent pseudo-anomalies. To address this, we propose PAR, a reconstruction-based framework for enhanced multi-view anomaly detection. First, we introduce a feature perturbation strategy coupled with a consensus aggregation module that integrates these perturbed features across viewpoints to mitigate view-inconsistent anomalies and construct highly aligned pseudo-anomaly representations. Second, we employ a view-specialized multi-decoder architecture; by assigning a dedicated decoder to each view, we increase the model's representational complexity, enabling the generation of highly feasible, localized anomaly maps rather than less precise, view-agnostic reconstructions. Last, we incorporate Fourier attention into the decoders to emphasize global frequency-domain structures over local token evidence, effectively preventing identity mapping. Extensive experiments on the Real-IAD dataset demonstrate that PAR consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods in sample-level, image-level, and pixel-level anomaly detection. Our code is available at https://github.com/SpatialAILab/PAR_mvad.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly used as automated judges for image-editing benchmarks, yet their reliability is poorly understood. Auditing these judges is difficult because real edited images lack controlled ground truth and entangle the requested changes with model artefacts, unintended side effects, and ambiguous degrees of success. In this paper, we introduce EditJudge-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark of 1,382 synthetic before/after image pairs rendered in Blender, with edits parameterised across nine categories derived from real user requests and exact ground truth from known 3D scene parameters. Because every edit is generated by a known intervention on a controlled scene, EditJudge-Bench evaluates VLM judges as binary verifiers of whether an instruction was correctly executed, isolating judge perception from editor quality. Across 25 judge configurations, we find substantial reliability gaps: (a) the widely adopted VIEScore template reaches only 69.0% AUROC with GPT-4o; (b) fine-tuned judges score 51.7-66.3%; spatial edits such as rotation and shape remain challenging; (c) prompt-formatting choices like image-text ordering shift performance by up to 14.1 points. Despite using only synthetic data, judge rankings on EditJudge-Bench correlate strongly with two real-image human-preference benchmarks ($ρ$=0.95 and $ρ$=0.71), indicating that controlled audits surface failure modes relevant to real evaluation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision language models (VLMs) encode substantial expert knowledge within their weights, yet their direct application to specialised classification domains such as benthic imagery remains limited. Meanwhile, vision transformers trained on these tasks rely predominantly on low-level visual latents (colours, textures, and shapes) that lack the higher-level reasoning humans employ for identification. We propose Guided Hierarchical Multi-Label (GHiML) learning, a pipeline that extracts conceptual latent variables from a VLM conditioned on training data, generates sample-relevant captions grounded in those variables, and fuses the resulting textual embeddings with image embeddings via cross-attention and a per-node gated router. We also introduce the inverted HML score ($d_{\mathrm{inv}}$), a similarity score that evaluates hierarchical multi-label predictions through graph traversal distance and a cardinality penalty, providing an interpretable alternative to existing measures, which are insensitive to realistic degradations in sparse, deep hierarchies. Experiments on BenthicNet, FathomNet and iNaturalist show that the router improves macro $F_1$ over the vision-only baseline by up to 6.7 pp and $d_{\mathrm{inv}}$ by up to 21.1 pp on in-domain benthic data, with gains persisting under distribution shift. Ablations locate the contribution of the text itself: on the multi-label biotic hierarchies, removing the per-sample text signal degrades $d_{\mathrm{inv}}$ while leaving macro $F_1$ unchanged, consistent with text acting through prediction cardinality rather than node discrimination, whereas iNaturalist, whose annotations are single-label, loses 18.7% of macro $F_1$. Our results support the hypothesis that VLMs contain exploitable specialised knowledge that, when appropriately elicited, can augment classification in domains where direct visual learning is insufficient.

</details>

Abstract (英文): As vision-language models (VLMs) move toward interpreting medical images directly, their outputs increasingly include not just a label but a stated confidence and a written justification. Yet the field still predominantly evaluates VLMs using just accuracy. We evaluate the coherence between answer, confidence, and explanation across four VLMs on breast density and BI-RADS categorization, using two mammography datasets. We perform controlled perturbations of the question, multiple choice option orders, and the images and assess changes in the three outputs. Our results suggest confidence does not decrease even as the mammography image is changed. Shuffling the options also changes most VLMs' answers. Notably, there are cases where a model's own stated reasoning contradicts its final answer, a distinction accuracy alone cannot capture but one that governs whether an AI-generated answer can be trusted.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Perceiving objects through fragmented occlusions, such as foliage, is a natural capability of the human visual system but remains a fundamental challenge for computer vision. This work addresses the problem of object reconstruction, or de-occlusion, in the presence of high-density, fragmented occlusions. We propose a reconstruction framework that operates on a sequence of grayscale images of a moving object, acquired by a single camera, either stationary or in motion. The algorithm estimates the object's relative positions across frames by jointly registering the images and optimizing the alignment parameters to maximize the contrast of their average, referred to as the integrated image. This integrated image serves as the reconstructed representation of the object. Experimental evaluations on both synthetic and real video sequences demonstrate the feasibility of accurate reconstruction under occlusion densities exceeding 90%. The proposed approach is competitive with synthetic aperture techniques, which are limited to static objects and require complex camera setup with careful calibration. Furthermore, we show that the integrated image enables reliable object classification under severe occlusion when used as input to standard, off-the-shelf classifiers.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sliced inference such as SAHI is the standard remedy for small objects in high-resolution imagery, but its gains are usually measured against low-resolution baselines. We revisit sliced inference with scale as the explicit variable and ask when tiling actually pays off. We compare seven dense detectors from four families on VisDrone and DOTA, under pre-specified hypotheses, calibration-only tuning and a single evaluation on held-out test data. Two findings emerge. First, much of the benefit attributed to slicing is a resolution effect: on VisDrone, a single pass trained at a higher resolution outperforms tiling with box fusion (merging decoded boxes) for every detector that could be trained at that resolution, and at equal compute a higher resolution helps more than a larger model. Second, tiles carry complementary information that box fusion wastes. We introduce SCAHI (Slicing with Coverage-Aware Hyper Inference), which accumulates the dense outputs of all passes with weights that give every image position the same total coverage, and reads objects off a peak grid fine enough to keep crowded neighbours apart. On identical passes, SCAHI outperforms box fusion for every YOLO detector on both datasets and matches it for FCOS. On DOTA, whose images far exceed the resolution a single pass can be trained at, tiles with SCAHI beat the best single pass for every detector, by 4.3 to 5.6 mAP50 at 2048 px, and replacing only SAHI's merge step by SCAHI adds 2.3 to 4.0 mAP50. An ablation attributes the gain to the border weighting and the resolution of the peak grid. Source code is available at https://github.com/beckerio/scahi.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Scribble-supervised segmentation is typically evaluated with a fixed annotation set, leaving robustness to distinct semantic and geometric imperfections poorly understood. Motivated by an audit of 12,031 manual PASCAL Visual Object Classes (VOC) 2012 scribbles and prior work, we introduce six controlled perturbation operators. Class corruption, spatial offset, and directional perturbation directly operationalize audit signals. Stroke-length reduction is motivated by prior scribble studies, while random dropout and fragmentation serve as diagnostic controls. Each operator reports requested and achieved changes. We evaluate manual and automatic scribbles across two image domains, two principal backbone settings, and multiple training pipelines, with an additional MiT-B1 PFA anchor evaluation. In the five-seed Tree Energy Loss (TEL)-ResNet101 VOC study, mean intersection over union (mIoU) drops from 70.08 to 1.46 under class corruption, compared with 35.57 for spatial offset, 52.20 for directional perturbation, and 63.24 for stroke-length reduction at their strongest settings. At the evaluated endpoints, semantic-anchor corruption produces the largest degradation, while geometric and support responses depend on the pipeline and dataset. Our benchmark provides a reproducible protocol for diagnosing annotation robustness beyond clean-set accuracy. The operator code is available at https://github.com/Wmhiram99/scribble-operator-benchmark.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent geospatial foundation models have introduced a new paradigm for large-scale Earth observation by providing pretrained embedding representations that can be directly exploited for downstream tasks. In this work, we investigate the use of Google AlphaEarth Foundations embeddings for national-scale crop type mapping in Morocco. The proposed workflow operates directly on publicly available 64-dimensional pretrained embeddings using lightweight downstream classifiers, eliminating the need to process raw satellite imagery during model training and inference. The approach targets three crop categories, namely winter cereals, tree crops, and other crops. For nationwide inference, the Fields of the World global field boundary map is used to constrain predictions to agricultural parcels, enabling efficient large-scale mapping. Experimental evaluation on an independent test set over ten repeated runs achieved an average classification accuracy of (87.17 +/- 0.42%). Nationwide inference over Morocco was completed in approximately five hours, with the dominant computational cost arising from cloud-based retrieval of the embeddings rather than model execution. These results demonstrate the potential of pretrained embedding representations for computationally efficient national-scale crop type mapping.

Abstract (英文): Medical images encode intrinsic tissue properties that indicate disease progression, yet these properties remain entangled with the system-dependent physics of image formation. Differentiable inverse rendering asks whether they are recoverable by inverting the imaging system's forward physics rather than through post-hoc correction. We address this in optical coherence tomography (OCT), focusing on the optical attenuation coefficient, a per-pixel marker of pathology and cellular composition. Conventional recovery relies on closed-form inversions limited to simplified light-tissue models, or learned methods coupled to specific imaging configurations. We instead recover per-pixel attenuation from a single OCT image by gradient descent through a differentiable physics-based forward model. We verified the method against analytical solutions, validated it on five in vitro phantoms, and applied it without modification to 29{,}269 clinical intravascular OCT images from 128 patients. The inversion needs no training data or segmentations and is portable across platforms and retrospective archives. Replacing the forward model or recovering another optical parameter leaves the optimization procedure unchanged. We demonstrate per-pixel recovery is tractable because gradient information scales to this high-dimensional problem, where gradient-free methods become intractable. Our work supports quantitative tissue characterization from OCT images and provides a framework for extension across imaging modalities and organ systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In production systems, 3D geometry is represented by primitives such as points, lines, and triangles, as rendering hardware is optimized to generate images from them very efficiently. Learning such primitives from images by volume rendering is challenging, as they are Dirac masses defined at infinitesimal locations in space. EWA splatting, Poisson reconstruction, and 3D Gaussian Splatting overcome this by convolving Dirac masses with a low-frequency kernel, a recipe that generalizes along two axes: the kernel and the primitive. As points and triangles have already received significant attention, we investigate this recipe for curvilinear primitives, and introduce Spaghetti Splatting. We approximate curves by polylines whose convolution converges to that of the continuous curve, and volume render each segment so that connected segments composite without overcounting at their joints. For fast training and inference, we linearize the camera projection per segment, which also accommodates kernels beyond Gaussians, such as Beta kernels. As a point is a curve of zero length, our representation is a strict superset of 3D Gaussian Splatting. It remains competitive with the state-of-the-art on general scenes, outperforms methods specialized to line-like geometry, natively supports anti-aliasing, and produces long connected strands that are directly amenable to physics simulation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Selecting an effective cover photo is a critical yet understudied problem in visual merchandising for e-commerce marketplaces. On Airbnb, hosts upload many photos depicting different rooms, amenities, and points of interest, but must manually choose a single static cover photo that is reused across heterogeneous product surfaces. This creates substantial challenges: over 90\% of listing photos have no engagement labels, leading to a severe cold-start regime; guest preferences vary across surfaces and search intents, and visually captivating photos may be semantically misaligned with the underlying listing. At Airbnb scale, this corresponds to hundreds of millions of photos and hundreds of thousands of new uploads per day.
  We propose \emph{OptiCover}, a scalable framework for cold-start visual optimization under semantic constraints and multi-surface engagement signals. The framework combines (i) a photo candidate generator that identifies candidate photos through various proxies, (ii) lightweight vision heads applied to frozen CLIP embeddings for large-scale semantic scoring, (iii) a cross-modal grounding validator based on a vision-language model (VLM) that enforces semantic representativeness, and (iv) an Exploration Logging Module (ELM) that orchestrates the collection of randomized engagement data across heterogeneous product surfaces and contextual search intents.
  We evaluate OptiCover through extensive offline studies and multiple online experiments across HomePage, and Search surfaces. OptiCover is now deployed in production and performs tens of millions of photo swaps daily. Online results show a 1.5\% engagement lift on the HomePage and a 0.35\% increase in bookers on Search, generating millions of dollars in incremental revenue. Our findings suggest that action-space shaping and semantic constraints are primary drivers of performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Modern digital phenotyping pipelines require the precise measurement of a range of 3D morphological plant traits. The accurate, high throughput acquisition of these traits helps breeders and researchers accelerate the development of new varieties, techniques and products. Recent advances in 3D technologies have enabled a rapid transition from 2D to 3D pipelines, but high throughput acquisition of digital 3D twins of plants remains a significant challenge due to complex morphology, thin structures and occlusions. In this paper we present Plant Orbiter, a new approach to generating 3D plants that leverages recent advances in video diffusion to produce high quality 3D Gaussian Splats of plants from a single image. We show that our method more accurately recreates plant morphology than other state-of-the-art methods, achieving the highest 3D F-score against all baselines, while being more practical for high throughput pipelines due to easier alignment and reduced cost of camera placement. Combined with a 2D diffusion model, we show that our method also achieves state-of-the-art results in the generation of 3D plant models from a text prompt.

Abstract (英文): Decoding visual information from EEG is central to brain--computer interfaces and brain-inspired intelligence, and contrastive alignment with pretrained vision foundation models (VFMs) has become the dominant paradigm. However, existing methods align EEG solely with CLIP-family VFMs, leaving hierarchical visual information in neural responses under-exploited. To address this gap, we formulate a mechanism-paradigm correspondence view linking hierarchical visual processing (from appearance to semantics) to the complementary biases of cross-paradigm VFMs, with image-only models (e.g., DINO) and image--text models (e.g., CLIP) capturing distinct facets of this hierarchy. Guided by this view, we propose the Dual-Teacher Hierarchical Alignment (DTHA) framework. Concretely, a shared EEG encoder is supervised by frozen multi-blur CLIP and shallow DINOv2 teachers via dual-branch contrastive alignment, so each branch fully supervises the visual cues it emphasizes. A fused-retrieval objective learns adaptive weights to combine their complementary visual representations for zero-shot EEG-to-image retrieval. For the shared EEG encoder, we introduce the Attentive Modulated Scalp-Pattern Bank Encoder (AMSPBE), combining temporo-spatial attention with a learnable scalp-pattern bank for accurate, interpretable visual feature extraction, while modeling regional temporal dynamics and filter-bank topographies. On THINGS-EEG zero-shot retrieval, DTHA achieves 92.4% Top-1 accuracy, exceeding prior state of the art by 12.4 percentage points. Spatial, spectral, and representational-similarity analyses, together with THINGS-MEG evaluation, validate DTHA's effectiveness, biological plausibility, and cross-modality generalization. This complementarity-driven perspective shows that supervision from cross-paradigm VFMs enables more complete extraction of hierarchical visual information from brain activity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Latent Diffusion Models dominate generative modeling but face two-stage training complexity and the inherent reconstruction limits of pre-trained autoencoders. Pixel-space Flow Matching offers a simplified end-to-end alternative yet often encounters training difficulties when using the standard $v$-prediction objective. Recent research attributes this difficulty to the off-manifold nature of noised targets, motivating a shift toward $x$-prediction. In this paper, we investigate this explanation through patch-wise Principal Component Analysis (PCA) and identify a bandwidth bottleneck. The velocity in minor components is largely determined by the raw noisy input, requiring deep backbones to preserve information that competes with principal-component modeling. Through controlled experiments and representation analysis, we further examine why $x$-prediction remains effective: its parameterization provides a raw-input bypass, while its learned input representation can spontaneously concentrate on principal directions. Under low-rank observations, our analysis of the induced flow characterizes $x$-prediction as an implicit conditional-mean decoder, reconstructing discarded components from the retained information. These observations motivate the Raw-injected Transformer (RiT), which combines a lightweight raw-conditioned decoder with an output-side shortcut to separate semantic modeling from raw-state propagation. Our method restores effective $v$-prediction and achieves competitive performance on ImageNet $256\times256$, providing a perspective on the interaction between prediction objectives and architectural bandwidth.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mature, germinal centre-bearing tertiary lymphoid structures (TLS) and mature lymph node follicles (secondary lymphoid structures, SLS) sustain the plasma cell response that shapes outcome and immunotherapy benefit in lung cancer. How this loco-regional axis differs between neoadjuvant-treated and therapy-naive tumors is poorly defined. We analyzed H&E whole-slide images of resected tumors and paired proximal and distal lymph nodes from matched neoadjuvant-treated (neoLUSC, n=20) and therapy-naive (naivLUSC, n=20) lung squamous cell carcinoma (LUSC) patients. Aignostics Atlas H&E Tissue Analysis (HETA) models quantified cell densities per tissue compartment and cell-cell co-localization; a dedicated deep-learning model validated against pathologist annotations detected TLS and SLS and graded their maturity. Prognostic value was tested in an independent cohort (n=458; 46 treated). In neoLUSC tumors, stromal plasma cell density was 72% (p=0.004) and stromal lymphocyte density 48% (p=0.015) lower than in naivLUSC; macrophages and granulocytes did not differ significantly. Total and immature TLS did not differ, whereas mature TLS in the tumor (p=0.034) and mature SLS in the tumor-distal lymph node (p=0.003) were lower in neoLUSC patients. Peri-TLS plasma cell density correlated with tumor lymphocyte density in naivLUSC (rho=0.54, p=0.017) but not in neoLUSC tumors (rho=0.07). In the validation cohort, stromal plasma cell density (HR 0.69, p=0.011) and lymphocyte-plasma cell co-localization (HR 0.66, p=0.004) were associated with longer survival, and neoadjuvant status with shorter survival (HR 1.50, p=0.026). Neoadjuvant therapy is thus associated with fewer mature lymphoid structures and weaker coupling of the humoral compartment to the tumor rather than uniformly lower immune readouts. The stromal plasma cell/co-localization axis is a candidate prognostic biomarker.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World action models jointly predict video and actions conditioned on language. Existing studies focus primarily on architectures and training mechanisms, typically treating language-video alignment acquired through video pretraining as an established foundation. However, introducing action learning may impair this alignment. Principled guidance on data and supervision design for maintaining language-video alignment during action learning remains limited. To investigate this, we curate a pool of approximately 47,000 hours of general videos, egocentric videos, UMI demonstrations, and robot demonstrations. Building on this, we systematically study how language-video supervision, language-video-action supervision, and the joint use of these two forms affect WAM performance. In particular, we examine how language annotation quality affects pretrained language-video capabilities, and how action supervision from UMI and robot demonstrations extends these capabilities to language-video-action modeling. To better scale the model, we further conduct detailed studies on joint training recipe and model architecture. Under a fixed training budget, mixing egocentric videos and UMI demonstrations improves control generalization. Model architecture affects how effectively these experiences are used, while scaling pretraining under the selected configuration brings further gains. The resulting recipe, post-trained on RoboTwin Clean demonstrations only, achieves success rates of 88.10% and 61.56% under Easy (Clean-to-Clean) and Hard (Clean-to-Random) conditions, respectively. Our model achieves 40.00% SR across three real-world tasks, compared with 23.33% for the strongest baseline, indicating better generalization under unseen conditions. We release the curated data, training code, and model weights.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Despite impressive progress, existing feed-forward large 3D reconstruction models produce a single, irrevocable prediction. When these models fail, there is no mechanism to correct their mistakes. To address this limitation, we introduce Click3R, a framework for interactive geometric correction of feed-forward models using sparse human correspondence clicks. Given an image pair and a small set of cross-view point correspondences provided by a user, Click3R injects geometric constraints into the 3D reconstruction networks through a lightweight point correspondence adapter, enabling the model to resolve ambiguities in challenging scenarios such as repetitive textures, symmetric structures, large viewpoint changes, or textureless regions. To evaluate interactive reconstruction, we curate a challenging benchmark specifically designed to expose failure modes of existing feed-forward 3D reconstruction methods. Experiments show that Click3R reduces reconstruction error with as few as a single correspondence click, improving performance on both standard and our curated benchmarks. Our results highlight that sparse human interaction provides an effective and practical mechanism for correcting geometric reconstruction errors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To interact with an object, we need to anticipate both how it will move and how our hands should move with it. Human videos capture these relationships across everyday activities, offering a natural source for learning to predict future interactions. Learning from them requires recovering motion from completed interactions and using it to anticipate what could happen next. We present $\textit{Grasping-the-Future}$, a unified generative model of joint 3D hand-object motion, including wrist motion, finger articulation, and object position and orientation. Through masked spatiotemporal completion and flow matching, we train a single model for several prediction tasks: forecasting future interactions, predicting hand motion given object motion or vice versa, generating both trajectories toward a desired final object pose, and completing partially observed interactions. We explicitly model hand-object relations and incorporate contact constraints to help preserve grasp relationships during motion. To obtain 3D supervision from internet videos, we develop a scalable pipeline that combines pretrained perception models with joint geometric optimization to reconstruct completed interactions. We combine these recovered trajectories with annotated interaction datasets as training data for all tasks. We demonstrate strong performance across all tasks, outperforming released forecasters on datasets with 3D annotations. Moreover, we show geometric and physical plausibility of the predicted trajectories by retargeting them to a robotic embodiment. Videos are available at https://grasping-the-future.github.io.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate information across modalities, but become unreliable when those modalities disagree. Under conflicting inputs, MLLMs may favor modalities that are not the most reliable for the task, thus motivating inference-time control of modality arbitration. We identify a small set of attention heads, termed arbitration heads, that causally influence whether predictions follow visual or textual evidence. Building on this, we introduce a lightweight, training-free intervention that biases these heads toward a designated trusted modality at inference time. The identified heads are stable across calibration set sizes and generalize across settings, and steering them enables effective bidirectional control of modality reliance across diverse benchmarks. Our findings identify an effective control mechanism to align MLLM predictions with task-specified modality priorities. The code is available at https://github.com/zhaoyingpan/modality-arbitration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Trained action policies write manipulation skill into network weights, tying what is learned to the distribution it is learned from. In-context learning (ICL) avoids this coupling but has not matched trained policies, because a demonstration recorded in another scene carries no feedback from the world the robot currently faces. We observe that what an agent learns in context is semantic, recording task steps and object roles rather than visual appearance, so experience gathered in one world transfers to another. Based on this insight, we propose World In-Context Learning (WICL), a paradigm in which a frozen model starts from a demonstration, rehearses the task in a world such as a simulator, and writes the feedback into its context rather than its weights. We build RoboScript, a modular infrastructure that implements WICL for agentic manipulation. From a single demonstration, RoboScript learns each task through rehearsal in simulation, freezes the learned knowledge as a Task Memory, and deploys the frozen memory on new scenes with data isolation that ensures every deployment input is available on a real robot. On all 50 tasks of RoboTwin 2.0, RoboScript reaches 83.4% mean success across clean and randomized settings with one demonstration per task and no gradient updates, exceeding the strongest trained action policy by over 20 points. The method generalizes across visual distributions, improves through reflection, scales with the foundation model, and transfers to a physical dual-arm robot through cross-embodiment memory adaptation. With a world in the loop, a robot can learn a manipulation task in its context as well as it learns one in its weights. All code is available at https://github.com/ArcheOne-AI/RoboScript

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) and multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) offer complementary routes to post-training. For visual agents, however, tool use presents two related challenges. Models may overlook useful tool-returned observations, while poorly grounded actions can limit the evidence available for subsequent distillation. We introduce Reframe, a post-training framework that treats the observation state, rather than only the output distribution, as an object of alignment. First, Ground-Drop-GRPO (GD-GRPO) trains spatial and temporal specialists with evidence-aware rewards and stochastic removal of the original visual input, encouraging them to acquire grounded evidence and use the returned observations. Second, Observation-Gated-MOPD (OG-MOPD) replaces deficient tool actions with canonical ones mid-rollout and resumes distillation from the repaired observation. The two stages complement each other, as learning to rely on tool-returned observations can help the policy benefit from observation repair. Neither stage requires a task-specific SFT cold start. Extensive experiments over 14 image and video benchmarks demonstrate that Reframe outperforms both Mix-RL and vanilla MOPD. Ablation results show that GD-GRPO increases observation dependence and tool gain compared with outcome-based GRPO.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Standing dead trees (SDTs) play a critical role in forest biodiversity assessment, disturbance monitoring, and carbon cycling. High-resolution optical satellite imagery offers an opportunity for large-scale SDT detection by providing finer spatial detail than moderate-resolution sensors. Despite this advantage, isolated SDTs may occupy only part of a PlanetScope pixel, allowing their signals to mix with those of live canopy, non-woody vegetation, and shadows. As a result, it is necessary to quantify signal detectability and detection stability across these conditions. To address this issue, this study uses PlanetScope imagery at 3 m resolution and Uncrewed Aerial Vehicle (UAV)-derived reference annotations to develop a multi-level, response-based SDT framework for analyzing SDT detectability from the aspect of the segmentation models, SDT density, and SDT patch sizes. UAV annotations are first aggregated to the PlanetScope pixel scale to generate hard labels and soft labels. The deep and machine learning models are then trained to select a stable reference semantic model and assess the consistency of SDT detection responses across architectures. All deep learning segmentation models produced consistent detectability responses, with HRNet achieving the best performance, with a binary F1 of 0.86. A single-pixel SDT signal became evident mainly when SDT cover proportion reached the 70 %-80 %, whereas neighborhood support lowered this change to a local SDT density of 20 %–30 %. Patch-level analysis indicated more stable detection for SDT patches larger than 90–180 m 2 . Furthermore, reliable PlanetScope detection is associated with SDT dominance, spatial aggregation, and high local SDT density . The proposed framework provides a detectability analysis approach for evaluating the stability of SDT detection in 3 m satellite imagery and the consistency of these responses across semantic segmentation models, linking high-resolution satellite imagery probability mapping with ultra-high-resolution UAV-based observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Polar clouds play important roles in regulating the surface energy budget, atmospheric moisture cycle, precipitation, and sea-ice variability, while their occurrence, vertical structure, thermodynamic phase, and microphysical properties remain difficult to characterize consistently across the Arctic and Antarctic. Satellite remote sensing enables broad-scale monitoring of polar clouds, yet high-albedo snow and ice surfaces, weak cloud–surface thermal contrast, frequent temperature inversions, and persistent mixed-phase conditions make cloud detection and property retrieval substantially more difficult than at lower latitudes. This review synthesizes progress during the quarter-century 2001–2025 in polar cloud satellite remote sensing with a focus on satellite-based polar cloud detection, cloud phase identification, and microphysical retrieval. It first analyzes the synergistic monitoring capabilities of multi-source satellite sensors, including passive multispectral imagers, active radars and lidars, and microwave radiometers. It then explores in depth the evolution of cloud detection methods from static physical thresholds to deep learning algorithms that integrate spatial information and multimodal features. For cloud phase identification and microphysical retrieval, this paper reviews the physical principles and technical limitations of representative methods, including threshold and Lookup Table (LUT) methods, Optimal Estimation Method (OEM), variational and Bayesian retrievals, analytical and empirical methods, and machine-learning approaches, with particular attention to the challenges associated with ice–liquid phase partitioning and vertical heterogeneity in polar mixed-phase clouds. Finally, it identifies the deficiencies in current research regarding the application of far-infrared and high-frequency submillimeter-wave bands, the construction of full-spatiotemporal coverage observation networks, and the physical consistency of data-driven models, and outlines priorities for developing a continuous, multi-dimensional polar cloud monitoring system based on coordinated high- and low-orbit observations, recent and forthcoming satellite instruments, and physically constrained artificial intelligence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate cropland change detection from high-resolution remote sensing images is essential for agricultural resource monitoring, land management, and food security. Existing methods struggle with two primary challenges: (1) severe pseudo-change noise and false alarms, caused by sole reliance on simple spatial or temporal difference enhancement, and (2) inadequate fine-grained cropland change detection, resulting from boundary detail degradation during fixed interpolation decoding. To address these issues, we propose a S patio T emporal D ifference- E nhancement N etwork (STDENet) with semantic flow alignment for effective cropland change detection from high-resolution remote sensing images. Built on a pyramid vision transformer V2 (PVT-V2) encoder, STDENet integrates three key innovations: a spatiotemporal dual-branch difference enhancement (STDDE) module that jointly captures temporal variations and spatial discrepancies to suppress irrelevant responses from unchanged or spectrally similar regions; an adaptive boundary-aware (ABA) module that dynamically extracts four-directional texture cues to enhance boundary representations; and a semantic flow-aligned decoding (SFAD) module that predicts semantic flow fields to warp high-level difference features and align them with low-level spatial details for fine-grained structure recovery. We evaluate the STDENet on one self-constructed dataset, DYCD, and two public benchmark datasets, CLCD and Hi-CNA. Comparative experiments confirm that STDENet consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods on all three datasets, improving IoU by 3.41 %, 4.90 %, and 1.98 % over the second-best methods on DYCD, CLCD, and Hi-CNA, respectively. By enhancing global structural information through the spatial branch and local change responses through the temporal branch, the spatiotemporal interaction design improves the feature separability of cropland changes. Semantic flow alignment helps restore fine-grained details by producing a more stable decoding process for local contours in cropland change patches. STDENet also demonstrates stronger resistance to interference and better adaptability to phenological variations among crop growth, pre-sowing, and post-harvest periods. Large-scale applications further indicate its generalization ability across heterogeneous cropland structures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurately estimating crop yields across scales (e.g., regional administrative and field scales) is critical for global food security, given mounting challenges agriculture faces from climate change, population growth, soil degradation, and water scarcity. Remote sensing has been widely used for crop yield estimation with its spatially extensive coverage and temporally continuous observations of crop conditions. However, robust yield estimation across scales remains challenging, particularly under conditions of limited availability of crop yield and crop management data. While process-based crop models can capture complex interactions of plant-soil-atmosphere continuum, their large-scale application is limited by computational cost, parameterization difficulty, and model input (e.g., management) constraints. Empirical data-driven models are more flexible and computationally efficient, but they heavily depend on ground-measured yield labels that are often scarce over space and time. To address these challenges, we propose PHYIELD, a novel hybrid yield estimation framework that integrates systematically calibrated process-based crop modeling and phenology-guided deep learning for maize yield estimation at both county and field scales under yield data constraint conditions. PHYIELD mainly comprises three components: systematic crop model calibration, diversified crop model simulation, and phenology-guided deep learning. Given the challenges and prevalence of sparse yield observations in practice, PHYIELD is uniquely devised with limited ground yield records for model building. Its model performance is systematically evaluated for temporal, spatial, and scale transfers, upon comparison with standalone crop model and deep learning counterpart benchmarks. Results showed that the systematic crop model calibration of PHYIELD, particularly via the calibration for Leaf Area Index (LAI), followed by yield (biomass multiplied by Harvest Index [HI]), is essential for region-varying parameterizations of the crop model before coupling with deep learning. With diversified simulations from calibrated crop model and phenology-guided deep learning, PHYIELD achieved correlation (R) of 0.53–0.73, bias of −1.47–0.96 t ha⁻ 1 , and root mean square error (RMSE) of 1.07–1.68 t ha⁻ 1 in the study region, outperforming both crop model and deep learning benchmarks. Under significant yield data constraint condition (e.g., only 2023 county-level yield data of Illinois available), PHYIELD demonstrated superior temporal transferability over the preceding 20 year period (2003–2022) (R = 0.70, RMSE = 1.76 t ha⁻ 1 ), spatial transfer to surrounding states (R = 0.85, RMSE = 1.06 t ha⁻ 1 ), and scale transfer from county to field levels (R = 0.53–0.76, RMSE = 1.55–1.58 t ha⁻ 1 ). By leveraging the complementary strengths of process-based and data-driven modeling approaches, coupled with systematic parameterization of crop model and incorporation of phenology knowledge, PHYIELD provides a more robust and generalizable solution for multi-scale maize yield estimation, particularly under limited data conditions. It can largely support yield-oriented decision-making in agricultural management, policy development, and food security planning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-derived observations of inland waters are widely used in environmental monitoring and climate research, often forming the basis of long-term analyses in the absence of continuous in situ measurements. However, the influence of transient atmospheric phenomena, such as volcanic plumes, on these datasets remains insufficiently quantified. In this study, we investigate the extent to which volcanic plumes influence lake surface water temperature (LSWT), turbidity, and chlorophyll-a measurements derived from ESA Climate Change Initiative (CCI) Lakes. Using a multi-sensor approach, we leverage MISR and MODIS observations to analyse plume–lake interactions during the 2011–2012 eruption of Puyehue-Cordón Caulle (PCC), Chile. Results indicate that while standard cloud screening procedures remove a substantial proportion of plume-affected observations, residual impacts remain. Notably, lake observations are influenced not only when plumes directly overlay the lake surface but also when plumes are proximal but not visually detectable over the lake, indicating the influence of diffuse aerosol particles. Analysis of aerosol microphysical properties demonstrates that particle-based classification can achieve approximately 67% accuracy, in identifying plume-affected observations but is limited by temporal variability in volcanic plume composition. In contrast, proximity-based approaches more consistently capture plume influence, suggesting that spatial context is critical for effective data flagging. These findings highlight a previously under-recognised source of uncertainty in satellite-derived inland water quality datasets. They support the implementation of proximity-based quality flags to improve data reliability in regions affected by volcanic activity in situations where consistent particle-based observations are unavailable.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate bathymetry in optically shallow waters (OSWs), particularly across coral reef environments, is essential for coastal ecosystem management and benthic habitat monitoring. Satellite-derived bathymetry (SDB) offers a scalable alternative, but reliance on in situ depth measurements constrains its application in data-sparse OSW regions, while the coupling between water-column and benthic signals limits retrieval accuracy. Here, we developed OSW-SDB, a simulation-based SDB model for Case-1 OSWs that is not tailored to a specific region and accounts for variations in benthic reflectance. Using a newly developed benthic reflectance library comprising 2,100 spectra, we generated 200,000 pairs of water depth and remote sensing reflectance (Rrs) by radiative transfer simulations spanning diverse water-column optical properties and benthic reflectance conditions. An XGBoost model was trained based on the simulated dataset using SHAP-selected multispectral features, with 6,711 matched ICESat-2/ATL24 observations additionally used for hyperparameter optimization. OSW-SDB achieved coefficient of determination (R2) values above 0.96 and root mean square error (RMSE) values below 1 m on the simulated training and testing datasets. Two independent simulation-based validations using benthic spectra excluded from model development yielded R2 values of 0.84 and 0.94, and RMSEs of 2.26 and 1.35 m, respectively. Median absolute percentage errors (MdAPEs) were below 15% across all simulated datasets. This model was applied to Sentinel-2/MSI imagery after OSW extraction, producing spatially coherent 10-m bathymetry maps across seven OSW regions. Independent validation using 13,296 ATL24 depths covering 20 reef sites yielded an R2 of 0.80, an RMSE of 0.75 m, and an MdAPE of 26.13%. Additional evaluations using airborne LiDAR and in situ SeaBASS observations, together with comparisons against two existing SDB models, demonstrated stable performance across geographically diverse OSWs. The model is designed for Case-1 OSWs shallower than 20 m, with better performance below 10 m, consistent with the depth range of benthic habitat mapping. OSW-SDB enables practical and transferable bathymetric mapping at 10-m resolution across diverse Case-1 OSWs using only Sentinel-2/MSI imagery, without requiring site-specific bathymetric or benthic priors during application.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-based wildfire spread studies differ in data organization and evaluation procedures, making model performance difficult to compare. We constructed an event-level benchmark to compare model performance and examine how the models used the available inputs. The benchmark covers California during 2012 to 2021 and combines VIIRS 375 m active-fire observations, MODIS NDVI-derived vegetation information, and ERA5-Land wind fields. Each wildfire event was represented on a fixed 100 × 100 grid. Each sequence sample used ten historical daily steps to predict the next-day satellite-observed fire-state mask. Six spatiotemporal models (CNN-LSTM, Vision Transformer, ConvLSTM, Pairwise-ConvLSTM, Patchwise-ConvLSTM, and ST-GNN) were evaluated using common input definitions, preprocessing procedures, prediction targets, and metrics. Integrated Gradients was applied after training to quantify attributions from the five raster input channels to the next-day model outputs. Pairwise-ConvLSTM achieved the lowest overall RMSE of 0.0495 on the cumulative wildfire dataset. Among the five evaluated Patchwise-ConvLSTM configurations, the 7 × 7 setting achieved the highest observed F1-score (0.955), with precision (0.962) and recall (0.949). On the newly burned-pixel subset, the same configuration achieved precision (0.916), recall (0.888), and F1-score (0.902). The attribution analysis showed larger mean absolute values for vegetation-constrained unburned states (7.23 × 10−5) and prior cumulative fire history (5.13 × 10−5) than for newly detected fire pixels (1.81 × 10−5), the eastward wind component (7.01 × 10−6), and the northward wind component (8.97 × 10−6). The benchmark provides a common framework for assessing predictive performance and model input use in next-day satellite-observed wildfire spread prediction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Drought increasingly constrains ecosystem functioning in semi-arid regions, yet the nonlinear thresholds through which vegetation restoration regulates carbon–water processes and ecosystem resilience remain poorly understood. In this study, a drought–carbon–water–resilience framework was developed to assess how vegetation restoration affects ecosystem drought resilience in the Mu Us Sandy Land, a representative semi-arid sandy ecosystem in northern China. MODIS products, including MOD17A2H/MOD17A3H for gross and net primary productivity (GPP and NPP), MOD16A2 for evapotranspiration (ET), MOD13A2 for the normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI), MOD11A2 for land surface temperature (LST), and MOD44B for vegetation cover, were used for the period 2001–2020 and harmonized to a 1000-m spatial resolution and an annual temporal scale to quantify water use efficiency (WUE), carbon use efficiency (CUE), the temperature–vegetation drought index (TVDI), ecosystem resistance, recovery, resilience, and a restoration intensity index (RII). The results showed that drought intensity increased slightly over time, while WUE and CUE exhibited contrasting spatial patterns and different sensitivities to drought. At the pixel scale, temporal correlations between annual TVDI and WUE/CUE were positive over 83.74% and 69.78% of the study area, respectively, indicating that carbon–water use efficiency often showed compensatory increases under mild-to-moderate drought conditions. In contrast, the regional drought-gradient analysis revealed threshold-dependent responses of WUE and CUE to increasing TVDI, with critical thresholds at approximately TVDI = 0.67 and TVDI = 0.66, respectively; beyond these thresholds, both water and carbon use efficiency declined rapidly. Strong restoration areas showed higher RSwue and RScue than weak/no restoration and degradation areas, and the proportion of high-resilience areas increased with restoration intensity, particularly for CUE-based resilience. Hierarchical regression further showed that RII provided additional explanatory power for ecosystem resilience after controlling for drought intensity, although its effects differed between water-use and carbon-use processes. These findings indicate that vegetation restoration enhances drought resilience through threshold-dependent carbon–water regulation, but its effectiveness depends on vegetation structure and water availability. Threshold-based monitoring and structure-optimized restoration should therefore be prioritized in semi-arid sandy ecosystem management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vegetation dynamics in drylands fundamentally restructure the land-atmosphere interface, yet the resulting surface water-energy coupling across saline-alkali environments remains insufficiently understood due to the complex interplay between climatic aridity and edaphic salinity. By integrating multi-decadal satellite observations with a biogeophysical decomposition framework, we quantified the growing-season impacts of vegetation enhancement (cropland expansion and grassland restoration) alongside desertification across the saline-alkali drylands of northern China from 2003 to 2020. Our results demonstrated that vegetation enhancement induced significant localized surface cooling, peaking in barren-to-cropland conversions (−0.674 ± 0.02 K), followed by grassland-to-cropland conversions (−0.066 ± 0.007 K) and barren-to-grassland restoration (−0.017 ± 0.009 K), whereas desertification exhibited a subtle warming tendency (+0.005 ± 0.0003 K). These thermal responses were primarily governed by non-radiative, evapotranspiration-mediated processes. Furthermore, the salinity-aridity nexus modulated regional energy partitioning through two divergent hydro-edaphic pathways. In reclaimed irrigated cropland, management interventions alleviated ambient matric and osmotic constraints, while intensifying regional water consumption. In contrast, non-irrigated restored grasslands exhibited a functional decoupling between canopy greening and evaporative cooling, reflecting conservative stomatal regulation enforced by compound soil matric and osmotic stresses. The marginal warming accompanying desertification indicated an impaired surface evaporative buffer following vegetation loss and secondary salinization. By elucidating how the salinity-aridity nexus modulates land-surface water-energy coupling, these findings highlight the necessity to align restoration strategies with local hydro-edaphic capacities, balancing the microclimatic cooling of irrigated agriculture against the long-term hydrological sustainability of natural grasslands under accelerating global change.

</details>
